PUBLIC WORKS:
 
a. Presentation on the Capital Improvement Program for 2025-2029:
Madhav Mundle stated that they started the Capital Improvement Program
last year and that this is the second of a five-year program. He indicated
that they are trying to make it better and more inclusive to reflect the
needs.  He stated that this presentation is to provide information on how
the projects were selected, that there are a few partnership projects, and
that one of the partnership projects is bound by an IGA and they are
required to have those funds.  He talked about the purpose of the CIP,
indicating that it is a roadmap for systematically constructing, upgrading,
and modernizing infrastructure; that it is a strategy for achieving goals and
objectives for near-term and long-term projects depending on the
priorities; and a plan for major capital needs over time. He stated that this
  
                                                                    2 of 16 
DRAFT

<!-- page 8 -->
is mostly a programming document and not a budget document, indicating
that the projects will be in different budget years.  He spoke about the CIP
development process, indicating that project prioritization is based on
need and available resources to complete the project and that the CIP is
reviewed and updated each year by Public Works staff, with the County
Engineer leading the process.  He stated that the projects are run on
revenue projects, that they are estimated at program levels and the
estimates will be tweaked as each fiscal year gets closer.  He indicated
that identification of a project doesn’t guarantee construction, that projects
may get deferred or dropped if the priorities change or the revenue
projections fall, and they will prioritize and determine which projects they
must deliver.  He spoke about the main criteria used for prioritizing
projects, including safety improvement, condition of roadways, drainage
improvements, and structure rehabilitation. He reviewed the various
projects for Engineering, Flood Control, Highway Maintenance and Fleet
Management that are included in the final CIP for the next year, indicating
that the document will be uploaded on their website.   
 
Supervisor Seymore shared his appreciation for the time that was put
forth to be able to see priorities they are working on and what is needed in
the communities.  He indicated that the hopes that upgrading to improve
the challenged areas will increase quality of roads in the county.
Supervisor Whitesinger stated that she appreciates the work, and talked
about the discussion held regarding some of the projects and being able
to better understand how they are prioritized.  She indicated that when
thinking about the amount of roadways we have, being able to understand
the road structures and the support that is needed is overwhelming.  She
stated that she knows that staff have worked hard to make sure they have
the appropriate data to be able to make decisions in helping prioritize the
projects to best meet the needs of the communities.
Vice-Chair Benally requested additional information on the District One
project. 
Mr. Mundle stated that there is a pilot project to slowly, based on the
priority, start to convert unpaved toad to paved roads by utilizing cold
recycled asphalt paving to improve the surface condition of the roadway. 
Supervisor Peshlakai shared his appreciation to Mr. Mundle and his
team for the providing the information regarding the CIP.  He thanked him
and his team for their continued support and work throughout the county.   
Chairman Whiting thanked Mr. Mundle and his team.  He spoke about
the study done last year to evaluate the roads, make determinations on
where they are at and how to best serve them, which has identified some
of the projects for the next few years. 
 
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to enter into the Navajo County
Flood Control District Board of Directors Session, motion seconded
by Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion.

a. Consideration of an Intergovernmental Agreement between the Navajo
County Flood Control District and the Silver Creek Flood Control District
for financial assistance for the Silver Creek Drainage Maintenance and
Improvements Projects in the amount of $20,000 per year for two years :
Donald Perkins stated that Silver Creek Flood Control District is requesting
financial assistance with their maintenance of the Silver Creek Drainage
Area.  He indicated that the purpose of the IGA to is authorize the County
to partner financially with the Silver Creek Flood Control District, indicating
that they share a common interest in improving the drainage area to
prevent future flooding.  He stated that Public Works has reviewed the
request and find it consistent with the purpose of Navajo County Flood
Control District.  He indicated that staff recommends approval. 
 
Chairman Whiting thanked Mr. Perkins for the work done on this,
indicating that it is important to the community members, that they have
been working on this for many years and that that it’s appropriate to
support and help them.
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to approve an Intergovernmental
Agreement between the Navajo County Flood Control District and the
Silver Creek Flood Control District for financial assistance for the Silver
Creek Drainage Maintenance and Improvements Projects in the amount of
$20,000 per year for two years, motion seconded
by Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to return to regular session,
motion seconded by Supervisor Seymore.  Vote unanimous approving
the motion. 
 
  
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:
 
a. Consideration and adoption of the proposed Tentative Budget for Fiscal
Year 2024-25 for Navajo County :
Jayson Vowell stated that this process starts in late January / early
February, and he expressed his appreciation to the various departments
for their cooperation and to the Board of Supervisors for their time in
meeting to provide direction and plan the next fiscal year. He spoke about
what services the County provides, such as promoting public safety
through the Sheriff’s Office, Jail District, County’s Attorney’s Office, Public
Defender, Probation, Superior Courts, Justice Courts, Clerk of Superior
Court and the Constables;  build and maintain county roads and support
transportation infrastructure through Public Works and the Flood Control
Department; support the health and welfare of the community through
Public Health, the Library District, Public Fiduciary, Emergency
Management and Workforce Department; provide planning,
building permit review, inspections and code compliance through Planning
and Development Service; provide mandated services, as required by
statute, through the Assessor, Elections, Recorder, Superintendent of
Schools and Treasurer; and supporting the functions of the Board of
Supervisors through Administration, the Clerk of the Board, Finance,
Human Resources, IT and Facilities.  Mr. Vowell spoke about the Mission
  
                                                                    4 of 16 
DRAFT

<!-- page 10 -->
Human Resources, IT and Facilities.  Mr. Vowell spoke about the Mission
Statement, Values and Vision Statement being part of the budget
foundation.  He stated that when looking at the budget foundation they
want to have a balanced budget, have financial reserves and fiscally
support the office and services through operations.  He reviewed the
highlights for the upcoming year as follows: primary property tax is levied
by the county for the general fund, that in 2024 Navajo County was the
third lowest in the state, that in 2024 we were the fourth lowest in the state
and that he feels the county is very responsible in assessing property tax
and what is assessed to property owners; that there were no general fund
positions added; that the general fund budget only increased
approximately 3.4 percent with the biggest increase being the contribution
to State ALTCS assessed by the State, and that the total budget increase
was approximately 1.4 percent.  He stated that there have been a lot of
one-time funds and that they do expect that the overall budget will start to
decline as projects are completed.  He spoke about the counties
compensation plan for employees, indicating that they are recommending
a 2.5 percent across the board cost of living adjustment and two additional
personal days; that there will be no increase to the cost of benefits; that
they have implemented a health savings account match to help pay for
medical expenses and an additional $500 one-time payment.  He
addressed the investments in regional projects that include broadband,
the Taylor Facility plan, the Winslow Levee, the Heber remodel, the South
County expansion and the GIS system.  He reviewed the proposed
budget, highlighting the general fund, special revenue fund, and capital
project fund, noting that the total of all funds is $251.6 million for fiscal
year 2024 and increased to $255 million for fiscal year 2025.  He spoke
about the sources of funds for the general fund which are state shared
sales tax; county sales tax; and property tax, penalties and interest,
indicating that is roughly seventy-seven percent of the available revenue. 
He highlighted the actuals and budgeted amounts for the state shared
sales tax and county sales tax, noting that it’s progressively increased
year over year and has been over the budget amount.  He talked about
the Joint Legislative Budget Committee projections for revenue, noting
that is has flattened out for the current year but they’re projecting in
FY2025 for that to rebound and then normalize in FY26/27.  He noted that
they are recommending that the primary property tax rate to be flat.  He
spoke about how the property tax is divided for the general funds, special
districts such as the Public Health District, Library District and Flood
Control District and the portion that is assessed for the school districts, fire
districts and community college.  He addressed the actual amount of the
primary property tax for the past several years versus the maximum
amounts that could be assessed, indicating that it has been less that the
amounts that are allowed to tax.  He stated that keeping a flat rate will
require that a truth in taxation public hearing be held prior to adoption of
the budget in July, noting that it is required by statute any time there is a
reduction or increase.  He reviewed the use of funds for the general fund
by function, which include criminal justice, public safety, mandated
services, county-wide services, capital outlays and Administration,
Finance, and HR/Risk, and by category, which include personnel and ERE
costs, supplies and services, Arizona State costs, capital outlay and
                                                                    5 of 16 
DRAFT

<!-- page 11 -->
insurance, software, legal and audit costs.  He noted for the secondary
property tax that they are recommending that it remain flat and talked
about the responsibility of assessment those taxes for the special districts,
which are used for specific areas to provide specific activities, such as
domestic water improvement districts, the health district, which is also
funded partly through grants, the flood control districts and library districts,
and the jail district.  He spoke about the projections for the Highway User
Revenue Funds (HURF) funds received from the state, used by the Public
Works Department, for transportation projects, indicating they are
recommending a budget of $11.3 million and for the vehicle license tax
they are recommending approximately $3.6 million and reviewed the
projects that those funds are used for.  He highlighted the capital projects
fund projects which include the Winslow Levy, ACA Broadband grant and
the Code Enforcement and Regional Communication project, noting that
there are some additional funding sources for some of the projects.  He
stated that the capital projects fund is one-time money and that as the
projects are completed the budget will decrease going forward over the
next couple of years.    
Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Mr. Vowell for the presentation and
shared her appreciation for the work that has been done.  She thanked all
of the departments for the work they have done to help ensure that the
county is providing the best services to communities.  She indicated that
the top priorities are ensuring that the mandated services are provided,
that the budget is balanced and that there is a reserve in the event funds
aren’t available so we can continue to provide quality services to the
communities.  She stated one of the priorities of the Board of Supervisors
and main responsibilities is to oversee the budget and make sure dollars
are used wisely to benefit communities and ensure the communities are
being prioritized.  She shared her appreciation for the amount of grants
that the county receives indicating that it is significant to be able to provide
the support and resources to the community members and for providing
the quality services. 
Supervisor Seymore talked about the difficulties with making everyone
happy, when looking at the budget and the different projects and having
enough to cover everything.  He stated that they understand that it’s the
citizens money and the citizens that they are trying to protect and keep the
rates as low as possible, noting that they have done a great job.  He
highlighted the Treasurer on amount that was brought in by moving funds
around.  He indicated that he feels that department heads are ambitious
in finding ways to save money and contribute to the success of the
county.  He stated that is amazing that they always find way to balance
the budget, both the state and counties, noting that they have had some
great legislative discussions to try and keep the state from balancing their
budget by taking away monies due to the counties.  He shared his
appreciation, indicating that this was a great summary and overview of the
budget. 
Chairman Whiting thanked Mr. Vowell for the presentation.  He indicated
that there was a downturn in the economy that was difficult, that there
were a lot of shifts that were made and that they had to find a way to move
forward as a county during a very difficult time.  He acknowledged the
efforts of the employees, department heads and elected officials as they
                                                                    6 of 16 
DRAFT

<!-- page 12 -->
looked to find ways forward.  He indicated that as the economy started to
improve, they continued to look at ways to be responsible so that once
things would be stable and employees wouldn’t have to experience the
same things they previously did.  He Thanked all of the Navajo County
team for hard work, noting that it is paying off.  He spoke of the property
tax being third lowest in the state, being able to stabilize as we move
forward and being able to try and help employees while still being
responsible to the citizens.  He shared his appreciation for all of the hard
work done by the Finance team and the other departments, noting that
everyone worked together as a team.  He indicated that one of the most
important responsibilities moving forward is to have a balanced budget.
Vice-Chair Benally thanked Mr. Vowell for the presentation and for
getting the budget in order again this year.  She shared her appreciation
to everyone involved and to Mr. Vowell for all of the work he does.
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to adopt the proposed

c. *Consideration and possible approval to discontinue the use of DS200
on-site tabulators and return to only using the central count tabulators:
Bryan Layton stated that they have received a lot of questions and
concerns, from citizens, regarding elections, the integrity of the machines,
and how the ballots are counted and transported over the past couple of
months, that he did a presentation to the Board and that this is a follow-up
to the presentation.  He indicated that they have received additional
questions regarding the machines, in particular the DS200 model, which
are the small tabulators that read and count ballots.  He stated that they
have fourteen of the units which are deployed to individual vote centers,
that citizens cast their vote on a paper ballot, feed it into the tabulator, that
the vote is counted, tabulated and then at the end of the night the results
are transferred onto a secure flash drive and delivered, with the paper
ballots to central count in Holbrook.  He indicated that there were a lot of
questions received regarding this, that they have a handout that can be
found on the county website under Elections-Information and FAQs,
noting that they are working on making it more searchable and easier to
get to.  He stated that there were a lot of very specific technical questions
that were received, that he reached out to the vendor, ES&S, that he
spoke with their Security Chief and that they helped with responses to a
lot of the questions.  He further stated that they have tried to provide as
much detail as possible in answering and being responsive to the
questions, which can be found on the website.  He summarized the
questions that they received regarding the machines, the first being the
capability for them to co communicate: do they have modems, can they
be compromised or hacked; and second the security of hardware and
software: who owns ES&S, where is their servers stored, what third party
partners are used and password security.  He shared the information that
they found regarding the different questions.  He stated as to the
questions regarding the capability to communicate that state statute
prohibits modem technology in tabulators and that selling DS200s with
modems in Arizona is illegal; that DS200 Tabulators can be ordered with
  
                                                                    8 of 16 
DRAFT

<!-- page 14 -->
modems for the handful of states that allow for secure modems and
software at an additional cost; and that ES&S provides instructions for
visual inspection to verify if a unit is equipped with a modem.  He talked
about the security or hardware and software, indicating that ES&S is
operated in Omaha, NE and there is no foreign investment; that all served
are owned, operated and maintained by ES&S in Omaha; that all
assemble of the units is done by ES&S in Omaha, computer chips are
from Intel and AMD, all products are transported by ES&S drivers and
vehicles and all steps in hardware production have chain of custody; all
software is written and developed by ES&S staff in Omaha and all steps
in software development has a chain of custody; that the DS200s use a
highly customized version of Windows 10 which is fundamentally altered
by ES&S, it’s a proprietary version that can’t communicate with or operate
anything besides ES&S, it can’t run MS Office and will not run .exe or
autorun riles, it’s can’t talk to other operating windows, is not susceptible
to “solar wind” attack, can’t access the internet and only DS200s with
modems can “speak” to other ES&S computers but not access the
internet, and DS200 has military-grade encryption tested against
“hashbunny” and other attacks; and that the third party companies,
including the Department of Homeland Security and academic institutions,
are used to test and validate. He Layton reviewed the takeaways from the
information received.  He indicated that from the county’s perspective the
DS200s have sufficient security and have been vetted and approve for
use by security personnel.  He spoke about the legal requirements found
in the first section of ARS Title 16, that ballots shall be counted by
automatic tabulating equipment and ballots may be counted manually only
if it becomes impracticable to count all or part of the ballots with tabulating
equipment and counties may not independently choose to do so.  He
talked about the guides and reference points clarifying that Arizona
counties must use tabulators, which include a letter to Mohave County
from the Attorney General’s Office and a letter to the Navajo County
Board of Supervisors from the Secretary of State, which can be found on
the website.  He spoke about how Navajo County is in compliance with
the statute by using the two machines located at central count.  He stated
that the DS200 machines that have been used for onsite tabulation at the
vote centers that have the most voter turnout for the past three elections,
that they have had some experience using them, that the county is in legal
compliance with ARS 16.  He reviewed the options for continuing to use
both the DS200 and central count tabulators and for discontinuing the use
the DS200 machines and only using the central count machines, noting
that both options are in compliance with state statute, and that it doesn’t
change the voter experience.  He stated that onsite tabulators may make
more sense in areas with larger populations but that they have turned out
to deliver the efficiencies that they had hoped for.  He indicated that they
feel that tabulating all the ballots at election central provides the best
environment for observable accountable elections.  He noted that
discontinuing use of the DS200 does not preclude staff from coming to the
Board in the future and recommend that they be used again.  He stated
that would move forward with either decision but that it is staffs preference
to simplify the process and use only the central count machines.

Chairman Whiting shared his appreciation for the time on this item.  He
indicated that there have been a lot of questions around this and stated
that he appreciates staff taking the time to listen to the citizens and learn
more about this, as well as the citizens being willing to engaging and
providing information that needed to be looked at and considered.  He
further stated that he appreciates Mr. Layton validating some of the
concerns to be considered if they were to continue to use them.  He
indicated that it’s his understanding that due to the structure here staff is
recommending just using the central tabulators.  He thanked Mr. Layton
for the time and work on this, noting that it is important to many community
members. He indicated that they wanted to make sure and look at this and
consider the options to best serve the people and be within the law. 
Supervisor Seymore stated that Mr. Layton reached out to the public,
answered questions and researched this thoroughly, noting that they can
and can’t do certain things.  He stated that by simplifying and moving the
process to a more central area that can be observed also simplifies the
long term that would have ongoing expense for maintaining and keeping
the machines up and that it will make the process more accurate.  He
thanked Mr. Layton and staff for the work on this item.   
Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Mr. Layton and staff, noting that it was
taxing going through all the questions, but that it is important to be able to
help the communities and those within the organization to best
understand the election and the steps we have taken as a county to
ensure we are following every regulation and law and that we are
accountable.  She stated that she is grateful to everyone who has taken
the time to answer the questions of the community members and going
through the process to investigate the best steps for moving forward.  She
indicated that it’s not something that they take lightly and that it’s best to
have deliberated on it and determine the best way to help the community
members feel confident in the election.  She stated that with every
question, if they did not know the answer, somebody reached out to the
organization to find the answers and that no question has been taken
lightly. She shared her appreciation to be able to better understand and
help the community member best understand the election process, the
roles and responsibilities governed by the law and be able to make
decisions that will best impact the communities so that no vote is
hindered, noting that every vote is important, and when looking at the
options the voter experience does not change.      
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to approve to discontinue the use
of DS200 on-site tabulators and return to only using the central count
tabulators, motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Vote unanimous
approving the motion.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. e.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: Contract Amendment No. 2 to Contract with eX2 for the Design, Construction,
Maintenance and Marketing of Fiber Optic Infrastructure
Submitted For: Grant Evans, Capital Projects Manager 
Submitted By: Grant Evans, Capital Projects Manager
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Contracts signed by County Manager/Assistant County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors
Resolution: Amendment No. 2 to eX2 Technology Contract for Design, Construction, Maintenance and
Marketing of Fiber Optic Infrastructure
Background:
On February 28, 2023, the Board approved a Contract with eX2 Technology, LLC (eX2) for the Design,
Construction, Maintenance and Marketing of Fiber Optic Infrastructure and authorized change order
authority to the County Manager for 2.5% of the System Price amount and authorized the County
Manager to execute the necessary documents.
This Contract Amendment No. 2 to Contract with eX2 is in the form of two change orders - change orders
numbers 3 and 4, both executed by the County Manager.
Change Order #3 is a no-cost change order and does not increase the overall contract amount.  This
change adds 'micro-trenching' to the contract as an available construction installation method.  This
installation method will be used to avoid culturally sensitive areas and, when appropriate, as an
alternative to mitigate against digging through hard rock areas.  Micro-trenching is more cost-effective
than the rock adder allowance included in the contract.  With this change, a portion of the rock adder
allowance is being shifted to the micro-trench installation method to provide contract capacity to utilize
the installation method.
Change Order #4 changes how the project will cross the Little Colorado River just south of Holbrook. 
This change will likely result in a net cost savings for the project, but ultimately will not be known until
after the work covered is completed.  The design and contract originally planned to bore under the Little
Colorado River crossing.  However, with this change, the contract value for the bore will be credited, and
we will now install our two conduits through an existing larger conduit on the bridge.  This work will be
completed on a time and materials basis.  
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY24 & 25
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested: 0
Fiscal Impact:
Change Order #3 has no fiscal impact and Change Order #4 is anticipated to result in a net savings to the

<!-- page 23 -->

Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: FY25 Family Counseling/County Match Request
Submitted For: Jason Cash, Chief Probation Officer 
Submitted By: Katherine Serna, Probation Finance Manager
Department: Probation
Motion before the Board:
Resolution Number 21-2024, approving the Family Counseling Grant budget for FY25 funding of
$11,810.00 and $2,953.00 in matching funds
Background:
This is a renewable grant that has been established for thirty (30) + years which pays juvenile counseling
for families.  If not approved, the County would lose additional funding from the state and would not
have money for family counseling for juveniles.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY25
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 2953.00
Fiscal Impact:
It is the recommendation that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors submit the County match of
$2,953.00 to the Probation Department in order to receive the Family Counseling Grant from the
Administrative Office of the Courts of $11,810.00 for a total of $14,763.00

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. i.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: Bourdon Ranch Road-Millet Swale Drainage Improvements - Road Closure (District III)
Submitted For: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer 
Submitted By: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Temporary road closure for Bourdon Ranch Road for up to 28 calendar days for the re-construction of the
new culvert at the Millett Swale drainage crossing
Background:
Navajo County Public Works has begun the procurement process to select a contractor to re-construct an
existing culvert on Bourdon Ranch Road, approximately 2.5 miles south of the Town of Taylor. During
the construction process, the roadway will have to be temporarily closed to allow for excavation and the
removal of the existing culverts, culvert system installation, headwall installation, and backfilling. The
selected contractor will be allowed to close the road for a maximum of 28 continuous calendar days.
Public Works has coordinated the closure with the Town of Taylor and will work with the selected
contractor to provide proper traffic control and detour information. Construction for the project is
expected to start at the beginning of August. However, the exact date of the road closure will be discussed
with the selected contractor and coordinated with the Town of Taylor to allow them to notify emergency
services. 
Additionally, prior to the road closure, Public Works will place digital message boards informing the
public of the temporary road closure so that the community can plan accordingly.

Title: Amendment No. 4 to the Contract for Hauling Waste Tires #B20-03-031
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment Number 4 to Contract #B20-03-031 Hauling Waste Tires extending the contract one
additional year from June 9, 2024 to June 8, 2025
Background:
On May 26, 2020, the Board of Supervisors approved the Contract with CRM of America, LLC (CRM)
for the Hauling of Waste Tires, Contract #B20-03-031. CRM was the only bid received, and this contract
has worked very well for the County. This contract may be renewed up to four additional one-year terms.
This would be the fourth renewal; the contractor is in concurrence and will renew with no cost increase to
the current contract. Staff recommends that the contract be extended one additional year (June 9, 2024, to
June 8, 2025).
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2024/2025
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in the Fiscal Year 2024 and Fiscal Year 2025 budget.

4894-4065-6832.3  
AMENDMENT TO REVOLVING CREDIT AGREEMENT 
 
(NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA) 
 
This AMENDMENT TO REVOLVING CREDIT AGREEMENT (this “Amendment”) is 
made and entered into as of June 30, 2024 (the “Amendment Date”), between NAVAJO COUNTY, 
ARIZONA, a county located in, and organized and created under the laws of, the State of Arizona 
(the “County”) and WELLS FARGO BANK, NATIONAL ASSOCIATION, a national banking 
association (the “Bank”). All capitalized terms used herein and not defined herein shall have the 
meanings set forth in the hereinafter defined Agreement. 
W I T N E S S E T H 
WHEREAS, the County and the Bank have previously entered into that certain Revolving 
Credit Agreement, dated as of July 1, 2021 (as amended, restated or otherwise modified from time 
to time, the “Agreement”), pursuant to which the Bank has agreed to extend credit to the County; 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 8.3 of the Agreement, the Agreement may be amended 
by a written amendment thereto executed by the County and the Bank; 
WHEREAS, the County has requested  and the Bank has agreed to make certain 
modifications to the Agreement, subject to the terms and conditions set forth herein; and 
WHEREAS, the County Treasurer is authorized by the Statue to act as the agent  for the 
County and accordingly shall act for the County with respect to its line of credit and any and all 
matters in connection herewith. 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the premises, the parties hereto hereby agree as 
follows: 
1. AMENDMENTS. 
 Upon the satisfaction of the conditions precedent set forth in Section 2 hereof, the 
Agreement is hereby amended as follows: 
1.01. The following definitions appearing in Paragraph 1.1 of the Agreement are hereby 
amended in their entireties and, as so amended, shall be restated to read as follows: 
“Commitment Amount” means, the amount of credit available to County, 
which is the lesser of (i) the maximum amount set forth on Schedule A attached 
hereto, as such Schedule A may be revised from time to time by County and Bank 
pursuant to Paragraph 2.1(f), or (ii) the Statutory Approved Amount; provided that 
the Commitment Amount shall at no time exceed $2,500,000. 
“Interest Rate” means the lesser of (i) the Prime Rate or (ii) the Maximum 
Rate. 
“Termination Date” means June 30, 2025.

<!-- page 52 -->
2  
4894-4065-6832.3  
1.02. Paragraph 2.1( f) of the Agreement i s hereby amended in its entirety and, as so 
amended, shall be restated to read as follows: 
(f) From time to time the Commitment Amount may be increased or decreased 
on the written agreement of the County Treasurer and Bank in accordance with this 
paragraph (f).  If County desires to increase or decrease its Commitment Amount, 
the County Treasurer (or its authorized representative thereof) shall complete and 
sign a Request for Modification of Commitment Amount ( the “Modification 
Form”) in substantially the form of Exhibit D hereto, and deliver the Modification 
Form to Bank.  If Bank consents to the modification set forth in the Modification 
Form (as determined by Bank in its sole and absolute discretion), Bank shall 
execute a copy of the Modification Form and return same to the County Treasurer.  
No increase or decrease of the Commitment Amount shall be effective until Bank 
has received the executed and completed Modification Form from the County 
Treasurer (or its authorized representative) and Bank shall have executed the same.  
Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, the written agreement of the 
County Treasurer shall not be required to decrease the Commitment Amount of 
County hereunder following a determination by Bank that an Event of Default (as 
defined below) exists, or that any condition exists that, after notice or the passage 
of time, or both, would constitute an Event of Default. 
1.03. The Agreement is hereby further amended by amending Schedule A in its entirety, 
and, as so amended, shall be restated to read as Schedule A attached hereto. 
1.04.  The Agreement is hereby further amended by the addition of a new Exhibit D 
which shall read as Exhibit D attached hereto. 
2. CONDITIONS PRECEDENT. 
This Amendment shall be effective as of the Amendment Date, subject to the satisfaction 
of or waiver by the Bank of all of the following conditions precedent: 
2.01. Receipt by the Bank of an executed counterpart of this Amendment. 
2.02. The following statements shall be true and correct as of the date hereof: 
(a) the representations and warranties of the County contained in Section 4 of 
the Agreement are true and correct, and are hereby remade by the County on and as of the 
date hereof as though made on and as of such date (except to the extent the same expressly 
relate to an earlier date); and 
(b) no Event of Default exist s (or would result from the execution of this 
Amendment), and no event or condition exists (or would result from the execution of this 
Amendment) that, after notice or lapse of time, or both, would constitute an Event of 
Default. 
2.03. All other legal matters pertaining to the execution and delivery of this Amendment 
shall be reasonably satisfactory to the Bank and its counsel.

3.81 Policy: (Revised July 2024)
3.8 PERSONAL LEAVE
A. Personal leave is provided to Full-time employees so that an employee can take care of
personal business which cannot be conducted before or after normal working hours. As with
other types of leave, use of Personal Leave requires prior Department approval
B. Full-time employees shall be credited on July 1 of each year with 32 hours of Personal
Leave unless otherwise authorized by the Board of Supervisors. Employees hired
throughout the fiscal year shall receive Personal Leave according to the following schedule:
MONTH
OF HIRE
HOURS
CREDITED
July – August 32
September – October 27
November – December 22
January – February 17
March – April 12
May – June 7
C. Part-time employees shall be credited for Personal Leave at one-half the hours above.
Temporary and seasonal employees shall not receive Personal Leave credits.
D. Personal Leave cannot carry over from one fiscal year to the next. Unused Personal
Leave shall be forfeited on June 30 of each year, or at separation of employment.
E. An employee transferring from one department to another shall retain any personal leave
credit.
F. Any request for accommodation of an extenuating circumstance outside the scope of this
policy will need to be approved by the County Manager.
3.82 Coverage:
This policy applies to all classified and unclassified positions. Nothing in this policy
modifies or waives the “at will” status of an unclassified employee.

6.9 TRAVEL RECONCILIATION
A. The Elected Official or Department Director or their designee should ensure:
1. That all travel expenditures are within their adopted budget.
2. The travel complies with the County Travel Policy and has been properly approved.
3. Adequate travel documentation is maintained.
a. A commercial establishment's original purchase order, invoice and receipts as applicable
are required for travel expenses including conference registrations, transportation, lodging,
and other miscellaneous charges.
b. Copies of all registration forms, meeting schedules, brochures or agendas shall be
submitted with the Travel Form.
c. Copies of purchase made with the County purchasing card can be included with the Travel
Form. Original P-card receipts are retained with the monthly P-card reconciliation.
4. The Employee Travel Form is properly completed and signed by the employee and department
official.
5. The original Employee Travel Form including supporting documentation is maintained by the
department. An electronic copy of the Employee Travel Form and supporting documentation
will be maintained on-line for audit and review purposes.
6. When a travel reimbursement amount is due to an employee a copy of the Employee travel
form will be submitted to Finance where the payment will be processed promptly through the
County’s accounts payable system. IRS guidelines require that requests for reimbursements
be made in a timely manner. It will be considered timely if employees receive advances within
30 days before the time that they incur the expense, and adequately account for the expense
within 60 days after completion of travel. Failure to comply with this requirement will jeopardize
the County reimbursement plan status as an accountable plan. Payments to employees for
travel and other necessary expenses under a non-accountable plan are considered wages and
must be treated as supplemental wages and are subject to income tax withholding and
payment of social security, Medicare and FUTA taxes. Any request for reimbursement received
after 60 days after completion of travel will be considered taxable income to the employee,
reimbursable through payroll, and be reported on the employee’s Form W-2.
7. Per IRS guidelines, excess reimbursement due to the County are to be repaid within 120 days
after the expense was paid or incurred. If amounts are not repaid, they may be withheld from
the employee’s bi-weekly payroll.
8. Non-reimbursable expenses cannot be claimed. The following expenses are examples of non-
reimbursable expenses: in-room movies, alcohol, and personal entertainment.
9. A fuel receipt is required to document the fueling of any county vehicle at any non-County
owned fuel station. The receipt must be attached to a completed “Fuel Receipt Documentation
Form” and be filed and submitted with your P-Card/Travel reconciliation forms.
a. Random Spot checks will be performed on Fuel Receipts to ensure all Policies are being
followed.

ORDINANCE NO. 03-11
JUSTICE COURT STAFFING FEE ORDINANCE
(Amended on June 25, 2024)
Amending Section 2 of Ordinance 03-11
WHEREAS the Board of Supervisors is authorized by A.R.S. §11-251.08 to adopt fee schedules
for specific products and services that Navajo County provides to the public; and
WHEREAS by the adoption of Ordinance No. 03-11, effective May 10, 2011, the Board of
Supervisors established a Justice Court Staffing Fee in the amount of $30.00 payable in civil,
criminal and traffic cases filed in the Navajo County Justice Courts as set forth in the Ordinance;
and
WHEREAS the fee imposed by Section 2.2 of the ordinance shall be reviewed by the Navajo
County Finance Department to ensure that the amount collected does not substantially exceed or
fail to defray the actual costs of staffing as set forth; and
WHEREAS the Finance Director has recommended an increase in the Justice Court Staffing Fee
from $30.00 to $48.00, effective July 1, 2024, to defray the costs; and
WHEREAS following a duly noticed public hearing held on this date, the Board finds that the
recommended fee of $48.00 reflects the increase required to defray the costs.
 SECTION 2: FEE TO DEFRAY STAFFING COSTS
2.1 Basis for Justice Court Staffing Fee .  The Justice Courts in Navajo County, the Navajo
County Attorney, Public Defender, and Finance Department have determined that
additional staffing is required to keep pace with increased caseloads resulting from
population growth in the county.  The fee established by this ordinance will not fully defray
the costs of such additional staffing but is based on the salary and employee-related
expenses for eight clerical and attorney positions serving the Justice Courts.  These costs
have been determined by the Finance Department to be $604,217 for the Fiscal Year 2024-
2025.  Based on current caseloads, the fee required to offset these costs is $48.00 per civil
case filing, $48.00 for each count on which the defendant is convicted in a criminal case,
and $48.00 for each civil traffic violation as to which a judgment is entered in favor of the

<!-- page 76 -->
state.
2.2 Fee.  Based on the analysis of the Navajo County Finance Department, there is hereby
imposed a Justice Court Staffing Fee of $48.00 to be collected in cases filed in the Justice
Courts.  This fee shall be collected as follows:  (1) from the plaintiff at the time of filing of
each civil action, with the exception of cases filed in the Small Claims Division; (2) from
the defendant as part of the fines, fees and surcharges imposed in each criminal action
resulting in a conviction, said fee to be collected on each separate count of which the
defendant is convicted; and (3) from the defendant as part of the civil sanctions, fees and
assessments in each civil traffic action in which a judgment is entered in favor of the state,
said fee to be collected on each separate violation as to which a judgment is entered in favor
of the state.  This fee may be waived only in cases of extreme financial hardship as
determined by the Justice of the Peace.  All such fees shall be deposited monthly with the
Navajo County Treasurer and held in a separate account for the sole purpose of defraying
staffing costs as set forth herein.
2.3 Annual Review.  The fee imposed by Section 2.2 shall be reviewed annually by the Navajo
County Finance Department to ensure that the amount collected does not substantially
exceed (or fail to defray) the actual costs of staffing as set forth herein.  If appropriate, the
Finance Director shall recommend an upward or downward adjustment of the fee to the
Board of Supervisors prior to the start of the next fiscal year.  Any such adjustment shall
be set forth in a resolution duly adopted by the Board.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County,
Arizona, Ordinance Number 03-11 is hereby amended on June 25, 2024.
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
 By _________________________________________
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman
ATTEST:
________________________________
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board
APPROVED TO FORM AND CONTENT:
___________________________________
Brandt Clerk, Deputy County Attorney

<!-- page 77 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING
 “NAVAJO COUNTY JUSTICE COURT STAFFING FEE ORDINANCE”
INCREASE OF JUSTICE COURT STAFFING FEE FROM $30 TO $48
Notice is hereby given that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors will consider at its regular
meeting on Tuesday, June 25 the possible adoption of an amendment to the Justice Court
Staffing Fee Ordinance (Ordinance No. 03-11) adopted in 2011.
The principal effect of the proposed ordinance would be to increase the Justice Court Staffing
Fee from $30 to $48.  This fee is charged to plaintiffs in civil cases and convicted defendants in
criminal and traffic cases in the Justice Courts in Navajo County to partially defray the expenses
of providing clerical and attorney staff.  The proposed increase is based on a cost analysis by the
Navajo County Finance Department. 
The meeting will begin at 9 a.m. in the Board of Supervisors’ Chambers, Navajo County
Governmental Complex, 100 E. Code Talkers Drive, South Highway 77, Holbrook, Arizona. 
The public is invited to attend and comment.  Written comments may be submitted before the
meeting to the Clerk of the Board of Supervisors, Melissa W. Buckley, P.O. Box 668, Holbrook,
AZ 86025.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: MOU with Institute for Intergovernmental Research
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Alyssa Lemmon, Public Health Nurse PT-PRN
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Institute for
Intergovernmental Research for the use of their free REDCap Data system
Background:
To improve the quality of data collection, analysis, and utilization, regarding the Overdose Fatality
Review and Suicide Mortality Review processes, NCPHSD seeks to enter an agreement with the Institute
for Intergovernmental Research for the use of their free REDCap Data system.  For the past several years,
we have utilized the ADHS Excel spreadsheet for data collection in these reviews.  While we will
continue to submit this required form to ADHS, utilizing the national REDCap database will allow us to
better track the data and recommendations generated during the review process.  This additional tool will
also assist us in identifying trends and themes to inform and advance the Overdose Fatality Review,
Suicide Mortality Review, and prevention activities with the goal of decreasing these types of death in our
community. IIR provides free training and technical assistance with the use of the REDCap data system.
All data will be de-identified and accessible only to authorized users.

 Memorandum of Understanding
Between the
Institute for Intergovernmental Research
and the
[Site]
for the
Overdose Fatality Review (OFR) Site
Permitted Use of OFR IIR-Hosted
REDCap Data Access Group
A. Purpose
The purpose of this Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is to outline the permitted use of all the data
provided and entered by the OFR Data User Site (Site) into the Overdose Fatality Review (OFR) Data System
database as part of the OFR Data System IIR-Hosted REDCap Data Access Group (DAG).
B. Background
The OFR project is multidimensional, utilizing the services of various partners and funding from the U.S.
Department of Justice, Office of Justice Programs, Bureau of Justice Assistance (BJA); and the U.S.
Department of Health and Human Services, Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC).
Role of the Institute for Intergovernmental Research (IIR):  IIR is the OFR training and technical
assistance provider and developed and supports the OFR Data System, including providing access via IIR-
Hosted REDCap DAG, training OFR teams to use the OFR Data System, revising the OFR Data System based
on user experience, and analyzing the data to identify trends and themes to inform and advance the OFR and
prevention field.
Role of [site] OFR Data User Site (Site):  Site is the entity responsible for leading the local OFR and entering
data into the DAG.
Description of Data to be Provided .  The data provided pursuant to this MOU contains data obtained or
created by the Site.  A detailed description of data collected by the Site can be found in the document “OFR
Data System Version 2.0 Guidance.”  The Guidance includes elements classified as Protected Health
Information (PHI), and therefore are not all captured in the DAG .  Only de-identified data is to be entered
and utilized pursuant to this MOU.  More information about PHI information not included in the DAG is
contained in section E. De-Identified Data and Appendix A.
The OFR Data System covers these broad areas:
 Decedent case review information
o OFR administration
o Decedent demographic information
o Cause of death
o Scene of overdose and death
o Drugs at the scene of death
o Death investigation and toxicology information
o Interventions following overdose
o Life stressors
o Health history and health care access
o Prescription drug monitoring program summary indicators
o Mental health history

<!-- page 80 -->
[Site]
2
o Substance use history
o Trauma history
o Criminal justice history
o Social service history
o Education history
o Recommendations summary
 County profile
o County health rankings
 Recommendation monitoring
 Next of kin interview
C. Authorized Users of OFR Data System
The data and information received by IIR from the Site cannot be provided or shared with other parties who
have not read and signed the Data Use Agreement (DUA) as outlined in Appendix B. An updated list of
personnel authorized to access the data will be submitted to IIR with any staff additions or changes. IIR shall
ensure that any subrecipients or subcontractors to whom it provides the data agree to the same restrictions on
use and access that apply to IIR and shall use appropriate safeguards to protect the data from misuse or
inappropriate disclosure of the data other than as provided in this MOU.
1. Authorized Users of Data Set.  List name, title and affiliation of all individuals who will have access
to the data and their roles in the use of the data as outlined below in Appendix B.  Only individuals
who have read and signed the DUA shall have access to the DAG to enter or view the Site’s data.
a) OFR IIR REDCap administrator.  The administrator(s) will be one or more IIR employee(s)or
contracted designee(s) who will perform administrative functions on the REDCap database
including adding users and setting user rights; overseeing modifications to the database; and
importing/exporting functions on data.  Some of these functions may be delegated to the OFR IIR
REDCap technician under supervision of an OFR REDCap administrator.  An OFR IIR REDCap
administrator will have full access to the Site’s data.
b) OFR IIR REDCap technician.  The technician(s) will be one or more IIR employee(s) or
contracted designee(s) who will assist an OFR IIR REDCap administrator in maintaining and
updating the OFR IIR REDCap database.  The OFR IIR REDCap technician will have full access
to the Site’s data.
c) OFR IIR REDCap evaluator.  The evaluator(s) will be one or more IIR employee(s) or contracted
designee(s) who will assist an OFR IIR REDCap administrator in evaluating the OFR Data
System.  The OFR IIR REDCap evaluator will have full access to the Site’s data.
d) OFR IIR data analyst(s).  The OFR data analysts will be one or more IIR employee(s) or
contracted designee(s) assigned to the OFR program under the supervision of the OFR REDCap
administrator.  The analyst(s) will have full access to the Site’s data for purposes of conducting
analyses and assuring data consistency and thoroughness.
e) Site lead/facilitator.  The Site lead/facilitator(s) is either an employee of the agency overseeing
the OFR or a designee and will have access to all of the Site’s data within the DAG.
f) Site data entry staff or abstractor(s).  The Site data entry staff or abstractor(s) are  responsible to
the local OFR lead/facilitator and enters data into the DAG from data sources supplied from
participating agencies.  The Site data entry staff or abstractor(s) will have access to all the Site’s
data within the DAG.
g) Site Data analyst.  At some sites, a data analyst will export de-identified data for analysis and will
have access to all the Site’s data within the DAG.
h) Site participants.  OFRs bring together personnel representing a range of relevant agencies with
data to contribute to understanding the causes of overdose fatalities and involvement in the
systems relevant to reducing their incidence.  Participants may provide data for the Site and
receive and discuss data on a case-by-case basis for an individual OFR.  Such participants do not

<!-- page 81 -->
[Site]
3
have direct access to the DAG but will receive data reported from it provided they have signed
confidentiality agreements ensuring that confidential information discussed within the OFR is not
shared outside the OFR itself.
i) Site MOU coordinator.  The Site MOU coordinator is either an employee of the agency
overseeing the OFR or a designee and is assigned to act as the communications contact between
the Site and IIR regarding data sharing and to coordinate and administer amendments and
attachments to this MOU.  The Site MOU coordinator attests that every member of the Site with
access to data covered under this MOU has read and signed Attachment B and that the Site will
require its staff to adhere to its policies and procedures regarding data confidentiality and
security.  The coordinator shall provide IIR copies of supporting documentation upon request.
j) IIR and Site agents.  The data and information received by IIR in the DAG from the Site
cannot be provided or shared with any other party, including agents (other than authorized
staff members) or subcontractors, who have not read and signed Attachment B.  IIR and
the Site shall ensure that any agents, including subcontractors to whom it provides the data,
agree to the same restrictions on use and access that apply to IIR and the Site and shall use
appropriate safeguards to protect the data from misuse or inappropriate disclosure of the
data other than as provided in this MOU.
D. Permitted Uses and Records Retention
The OFR data entered in the DAG by the Site must be de-identified to exclude personally identifiable
information as outlined in Appendix A.
IIR will be given access, for the duration of the MOU, to all the de-identified data provided by the Site to the
DAG for only the purpose described above.  Any additional use of the data, or modification of the use of the
data, is prohibited and will require an amendment to the MOU.
IIR will not use or further disclose data or information provided pursuant to this MOU other than as permitted
by this MOU.  Disclosure of records related to this effort in the custody of a Site sought under the state public
records law of the Site may also be sought.  Each Site is responsible for conforming with applicable public
record laws and should utilize appropriate exemptions from disclosure as applicable to protect the confidential
nature of the stored records in its possession.
Should any request for records related to this effort be received by a Site under its applicable public records
law, the recipient of a disclosure request shall notify IIR to discuss whether any applicable exemptions to
disclosure  under the law may be asserted.  IIR will notify a Site if a request for disclosure of Site records is
received and discuss the response to be made, if any.
The only external data that may be linked to the individual de-identified data will be OFR structure
information available from the “register your OFR” survey tool and or publicly available population-based
data such as community-level overdose death rates and other social determinant/public health measures.
IIR will release data obtained through this effort in its possession only in response to a valid court order or
other legal mandate.
E. De-Identified Data
Only de-identified data will be utilized pursuant to this MOU . If submitted data has not been 
de-identified  the data shall not be utilized and must be kept secured          and promptly de-identified by the
Site. Should information that has not been de-identified be received by IIR, it will be returned to the
contributor/Site for de-identification.
All fields related to personally identifiable information will be excluded from data entry.  Data elements not

<!-- page 82 -->
[Site]
4
shared with IIR are specified in Appendix A. All cases are being assigned a unique random identifier within
REDCap that will be used in lieu of identifying information.
F. Safeguards
IIR shall use appropriate administrative, physical, and technical safeguards to prevent the use or disclosure of
data other than as provided by this MOU.  Data will be shared in a secure manner and will only be stored on
a secure server or encrypted device.  In accord with federal grant conditions, each Site participating in this
MOU must maintain its records related to such participation for a minimum of three years.  A summary of
federal obligations is provided in Appendix C.
The REDCap data system includes built-in functions to facilitate control of access to data.  All cases will be
assigned a unique random identifier within REDCap that will be used in lieu of identifying information.
G. Staff Confidentiality Acknowledgement
IIR will limit its staff’s and designees’ access to only those necessary to accomplish the purposes identified
in this MOU.  IIR attests that all staff and contracted designees with access to the data under this MOU have
read and agreed to abide by the DUA included in Appendix B.  Staff will be required to adhere to the
confidentiality and security restrictions included in this MOU and the DUA.  These confidentiality restrictions
survive if the signer terminates employment with the signer’s employer.
H. Reporting Unauthorized Disclosures
IIR shall report to the Site MOU coordinator within two business days upon becoming aware of any actual or
attempted use or disclosure of information submitted by the Site not authorized by this MOU or applicable law,
and steps being taken by IIR in response to the incident. Within two business days, a Site MOU coordinator
shall advise IIR of any actual or attempted use or disclosure of information not authorized by this MOU or
applicable law and indicate what steps are being taken at the Site in response to the incident.
I. Data Ownership
IIR acknowledges that all data saved on the DAG shall be and remain the sole property of the originating Site.
The data provided may not be used or disclosed for any purpose other  than as outlined in this MOU or as
otherwise required by law.
If any such information falls within the potential scope of a request for record disclosure received by IIR, the
Site of the information shall be consulted prior to any such disclosure to allow an opportunity to claim
exemption   from disclosure or otherwise seek judicial intervention to prevent such disclosure.
J. Notification and Review Procedures for Publication and Presentations
IIR staff members and designees who have access to the data and information provided from the Site shall not
publicly present or submit for publication—in electronic form, oral form, or in writing on a formal or informal
basis—subjects or research that used the data and information obtained under this MOU without the prior
review by the Site MOU coordinator if the publication specifically pertains to or can be attributed to the Site.
Use of de-identified data and information in aggregate across multiple Sites does not require review by each
Site but does require approval from IIR.
“Publication” includes any electronic or written article for publication in a professional journal or other
publication or any electronic or printed material for presentation to any person, conference, or group external
to IIR.  This includes providing copies of planned articles, the text of speeches, slides, and other graphics.

<!-- page 83 -->
[Site]
5
General requirements for federal or IIR approval of publication as stated in the MOU are to be adhered to.
All publications utilizing data or information covered by this MOU shall be reviewed by IIR to ensure
consistency, to avoid inappropriate disclosure, and to promote the goal of the project.
Unless otherwise mutually agreed upon between the parties, IIR shall provide the Site MOU coordinator with
a minimum of 20 working days for the first review of such presentations (e.g., abstracts, slides) and
publications and 10 working days for any subsequent reviews.
Any presentations, reports, and research articles or drafts of any of these, which are based on data covered by
this MOU, may present data in de-identified aggregate form only. No aggregate information that would enable
the direct or indirect identification of an individual may be published or distributed in any form.
K. Relationship of Parties
The Site and IIR are independent parties and nothing in this MOU shall make either party the agent or legal
representative of the other for any purpose whatsoever, nor does it grant either party any authority  to assume
or to create any obligation on behalf of or in the name of the other.
L. Communication
Communication is essential to ensure the successful operation and management of this project. The parties
agree to maintain open lines of communication between designated staff members at appropriate managerial
and technical levels.
M. Jurisdiction and Settlement of Disputes
All parties agree to abide by all applicable local, state, tribal, and federal laws pertaining to the authorized
collection, proper use, retention, and secure dissemination of OFR information.
Disagreements between the parties arising under or relating to this MOU will be resolved only by consultation
between the parties to this MOU and their designees or appointed representatives.
N. Effect of This MOU
This MOU is not an obligation or commitment of funds, nor a basis for transfer of funds, but rather is a basic
statement of the understanding between the parties of the matters described herein.  Expenditures by  each
party will be subject to its budgetary processes and to the availability of funds and resources pursuant  to
applicable laws, regulations, and policies.  The Site and IIR expressly acknowledge the language in this MOU
in no way implies that funds will be made available for such expenditures.
This MOU does not constitute an agreement for any party to assume or waive any liability or claim under any
applicable law.
O. Effective Date, Administration, and Termination
This MOU may be modified at any time , by written consent , of the parties.  Any modification shall be
conveyed to affected persons to assure compliance with new obligations, responsibilities, or terms.
This MOU may be terminated at any time upon written notice of withdrawal to the other party provided at
least thirty (30) days prior to withdrawal.
Each provision of this MOU is severable.  If one provision is declared void, illegal, or unenforceable, the
remaining paragraphs shall retain their full force and effect.

Appendix C – Summary of Records Retention Obligations
Excerpt From CFR 200 – Uniform Guidance
§200.334 Retention requirements for records.
Financial records, supporting documents, statistical records, and all other non-Federal
entity records pertinent to a Federal award must be retained for a period of three years from the
date of submission of the final expenditure report or, for Federal awards that are renewed
quarterly or annually, from the date of the submission of the quarterly or annual financial report,
respectively, as reported to the Federal awarding agency or pass-through entity in the case of a
subrecipient. Federal awarding agencies and pass-through entities must not impose any other record
retention requirements upon non-Federal entities. The only exceptions are the following:
(a) If any litigation, claim, or audit is started before the expiration of the 3-year period, the
records must be retained until all litigation, claims, or audit findings involving the records have been
resolved and final action taken.
(b) When the non-Federal entity is notified in writing by the Federal awarding agency, cognizant
agency for audit, oversight agency for audit, cognizant agency for indirect costs, or pass-through entity
to extend the retention period.
(c) Records for real property and equipment acquired with Federal funds must be retained for 3
years after final disposition.
(d) When records are transferred to or maintained by the Federal awarding agency or pass-
through entity, the 3-year retention requirement is not applicable to the non-Federal entity.
(e) Records for program income transactions after the period of performance. In some cases
recipients must report program income after the period of performance. Where there is such a
requirement, the retention period for the records pertaining to the earning of the program income starts
from the end of the non-Federal entity's fiscal year in which the program income is earned.
(f) Indirect cost rate proposals and cost allocations plans. This paragraph applies to the following
types of documents and their supporting records: Indirect cost rate computations or proposals, cost
allocation plans, and any similar accounting computations of the rate at which a particular group of
costs is chargeable (such as computer usage chargeback rates or composite fringe benefit rates).
(1) If submitted for negotiation. If the proposal, plan, or other computation is required to be
submitted to the Federal Government (or to the pass-through entity) to form the basis for negotiation of
the rate, then the 3-year retention period for its supporting records starts from the date of such
submission.
(2) If not submitted for negotiation. If the proposal, plan, or other computation is not required to
be submitted to the Federal Government (or to the pass-through entity) for negotiation purposes, then
the 3-year retention period for the proposal, plan, or computation and its supporting records starts from
the end of the fiscal year (or other accounting period) covered by the proposal, plan, or other
computation.

<!-- page 88 -->
[Site]
10
Excerpt from the DOJ Financial Guide
3.16 RETENTION AND ACCESS REQUIREMENTS FOR RECORDS
Retention of Records
Retain all financial records, supporting documents, statistical records, and all other records pertinent to the
award for a period of 3 years from the date of submission of the final expenditure report (Federal Financial
Report/SF-425). For more information, see 2 C.F.R. § 200.333.
■ Retention is required for purposes of Federal examination and audit.
■ Records may be retained in an electronic format.
Coverage
The retention requirement includes, if applicable, books of original entry, source documents, supporting
accounting transactions, the general ledger, subsidiary ledgers, personnel and payroll records, cancelled
checks, and related documents and records.
■ Source documents may include copies of all awards, applications, and required recipient financial and
narrative reports.
■ Personnel and payroll records must include the time and attendance reports for all full-time and/or part-
time individuals reimbursed under the award.
■ Time and effort reports are also required for consultants.
Exception to the 3-Year Retention Period
The 3-year retention period starts from the date of submission of the final expenditure report. The following
are exceptions to the standard record retention period:
■ If any litigation, claim, audit, or other action involving the records has started before the expiration of
the3-year period, the records must be retained until all issues involving the records have been resolved
and final action taken.
■ When notified by the DOJ grant-making component, cognizant agency for audit, oversight agency for
audit, cognizant agency for indirect costs, or pass-through entity to extend the retention period.
■ Records for real property and equipment acquired with Federal funds must be retained for 3 years after
the final disposition.
■ When records are transferred to or maintained by the DOJ grant-making component or pass-through
entity, the 3 year retention period requirement is not applicable to the non-Federal entity.
■ When required for program income earned after the period of performance, the retention period starts
from the end of the non-Federal entity’s fiscal year in which the program income is earned.
■ Indirect cost proposals submitted for negotiation must be retained for 3 years from the date of submission.
■ Indirect cost proposals not required to be submitted for negotiation must be maintained for 3 years from
the end of the fiscal year covered by the proposal.
Maintenance of Records
Maintain and separately identify all records for each Federal fiscal period so that information desired may
be readily located.
■ Protect the records adequately against fire or other damage.
■ Store the records away from the recipient’s principal office; however, a list of the documents must be
available if needed.
■ Whenever practicable, information should be collected, transmitted and stored in open and machine
readable formats rather than in closed formats or on paper.

<!-- page 89 -->
[Site]
11
Access to Records
The DOJ grant-making component, the DOJ Office of the Inspector General, the Comptroller General of the
United States, and the pass-through entity, or any of their authorized representatives, must have access to
any documents, papers, or other records of recipients which are pertinent to the award, in order to make
audits, examinations, excerpts, and transcripts.
■ The right of access is not limited to the required retention period; it will last as long as the records are
retained.
■ However, only under extraordinary and rare circumstances would such access include review of
information that would personally identify confidential informants or victims of crime. Routine
monitoring cannot be considered extraordinary and rare circumstances that would necessitate access to
this information. When access to the information that could personally identify victims of a crime is
determined to be necessary, appropriate steps to protect this sensitive information must be taken by both
the non-Federal entity and the DOJ grant-making component.
■ Any such access, other than under a court order or subpoena pursuant to a bona fide confidential
investigation, must be approved by the Head of the DOJ grant-making component.
(end)

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. b. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: Ordinance 01-24 Animal Care and Control Ordinance
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Brandon Damron, Animal Care & Control Officer II
Department: NC Public Health Services District Division: Code Enforcement Division
Presented By: Rhonda Krouse
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Ordinance Number 01-24, repealing all other
ordinances concerning animal care and control and adopting this comprehensive Ordinance. 
Background:
The Navajo County Animal Care and Control Department provides important services to our community
by educating animal owners about the most appropriate way of handling their animals. The Animal Care
and Control Department is also required by State Statue to enforce animal laws. The Navajo County
Animal Care and Control Department also proposes to update the associated fee schedule for these
services. The fee schedule has not been updated since 2006 and fees no longer reflect the current cost to
deliver services. The fees assessed to the public in violation of the ordinance will help offset the cost of
performing these duties.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 24/25
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
There will be an increase in the budget with the amended fees. We are estimating approximately a $5,000
increase.
