Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 1
Ordinance 01-24
Navajo County Animal Care and Control Ordinance
An Ordinance of the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County, Arizona, repealing all other ordinances
concerning this subject matter and adopting this comprehensive Ordinance.
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Animal Care & Control Division incorporates all other animal/dog control
ordinances herein; and
WHEREAS, upon adoption of this Ordinance, all other ordinances concerning this subject matter shall be
repealed; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, after considering the testimony and other evidence
presented at the public hearing, finds that the “Navajo County Animal Care and Control Ordinance” is
consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should be adopted as set forth herein.
NOW, THERFORE, BE IT ORDAINED by the Board of Supervisors that the Navajo County Animal Care
and Control Ordinance is adopted as follows:
Article 1 – General Provisions
1.1 Purpose
The purpose of this Ordinance is to comprehensively regulate the keeping of animals within the unincorporated
area of Navajo County, Arizona.
1.2 Short title
This Ordinance may be cited as the "Navajo County Animal Control Ordinance."
1.3 Applicability
This Ordinance applies within the unincorporated area of Navajo County, except for land under the jurisdiction
of a federally recognized Tribal Land.
1.4. Authority
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors is authorized by A.R.S §11-251(47) to "make and enforce ordinances
for the protection and disposition of domestic animals subject to inhumane, unhealthful or dangerous conditions
or circumstances."
The Board of Supervisors is further authorized by Title 11, Chapter 7, Article 6 of the Arizona Revised Statutes
(A.R.S §11-1001 et seq.) to appoint a County Enforcement Agent, to require the licensing of dogs, and to
regulate, restrain and prohibit the running at large of dogs and the excessive and unrestrained barking of dogs
by the adoption of ordinances at least as stringent as the state statutes.
All provisions of Title 11, Chapter 7, Article 6 of the Arizona Revised Statutes, as the same may be amended
from time to time, are hereby incorporated by this reference and shall hereinafter be referred to as the "State
Animal Control Statutes." If there is any inconsistency between this Ordinance and a provision of the State
Animal Control Statutes, the statutory provision shall control.

<!-- page 105 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 2
1.5 Repeal of prior ordinances
This Ordinance supersedes all ordinances of Navajo County concerning the same subject matter, including
without limitation Ordinance 01-86, Dog Control Ordinance, Regulate and Prohibit the running at large dogs
within the unincorporated areas of Navajo County, adopted May 20, 1986; Ordinance 01-93, Navajo County
Animal Control Ordinance, to include previous Ordinance 01-86 and adopt Animal Control State Statutes,
which includes dogs at large adopted on March 8, 1993; Ordinance 01-93, revised May 17, 1997 and
approved July 7, 1997, to adopt a fee schedule for the White Mountain and Winslow Humane Society;
Ordinance 02-06, Regulating the Keeping of Animals within the unincorporated area of Navajo County and
providing criminal penalties for violations, adopted June 5, 2006. This ordinance encompasses the previous
ordinance of adopting the Animal Control State Statutes and dogs at large; Ordinance 01-08, Prohibiting the
release into the wild of certain predatory animals with specific criminal penalties, adopted on May 6, 2008;
Ordinance 04-10, Remedial actions to avoid contact between humans and bears, coyotes, or javelinas, was
added as a new article to Ordinance 02-06 and adopted May 11, 2010; Ordinance 02-13, Predatory Animal
Ordinance to amend Ordinance 01-08 by amending Article 6-Enforcement, adopted September 24, 2013.
1.6 Rabies Control Fund
The County Treasurer shall place the monies collected under the provisions of this Ordinance in a special fund
to be known as the Rabies Control Fund to be used for the enforcement of the provisions of this Ordinance
pursuit to A.R.S §11-1011. Any unencumbered balance remaining in the Rabies Control Fund at the end of a
fiscal year shall be carried over into the following fiscal year.
1.7 Severability
If any provision of this Ordinance is held to be unenforceable by a court of competent jurisdiction, the
unenforceable provision shall be stricken, and the remaining provisions shall remain in full force and effect.
1.8 Definitions
“Animal” means any fowl, reptile, amphibian, or mammal, except for human beings.
"Attack" means, with respect to animals and this ordinance, to bite, attempt to bite, growl, and show teeth,
charge, chase, jump on, scratch, kick or otherwise act in a threatening or aggressive manner which animal act
puts a person in fear of being harmed or injured, or which unreasonably interferes with that person’s ability to
carry on lawful activities. No actual touching or biting need occur for an "attack" to take place. The finder of
fact shall use a "totality of the circumstances" analysis to determine whether an "attack" has occurred. For
example, a dog which jumps a fence and chases a person walking to the neighborhood mailbox where the facts
indicate that the person is justifiably threatened or scared by the dog’s behavior has been "attacked."
"At large" means on or off the premises of an owner and either:
 not under the control of the owner by a leash, cord, rope, chain, or other physical restraint not more than
six feet in length and of sufficient strength to effectively control the dog; or
 not enclosed within a suitable enclosure.
"Board" means the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County.
"County Pound" means any establishment authorized by the Board for the confinement, maintenance,
safekeeping and control of dogs and other animals which come into the custody of the County Enforcement
Agent in the performance of the agent’s official duties.
“County Enforcement Agent” means a Navajo County Animal Care & Control Officer.
"Cruelty" means any act of omission whereby unjustifiable physical pain, suffering or death of an animal is
caused or permitted, including failure to provide proper drink, air, space, shelter or protection from the
elements, a sanitary and safe living environment, veterinary care, or nutritious food in sufficient quantity.

<!-- page 106 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 3
“Dog” means any animal of the canine species; including wolf-hybrids (see Section 2.1.1 for special rule
applicable to wolf-hybrids).
“Exotic Animal” means any live monkey, alligator, crocodile, Cayman, raccoon, skunk, fox, bear, sea mammal,
poisonous snake, member of the feline species other than domestic cat (felis domesticus), member of the canine
species other than domestic dog (canis familiaris) or any other animal that would require a standard of care and
control greater than that required for customary household pets sold by commercial pet shops or domestic farm
animals.
“Fowl” means a bird of any kind.
“Livestock" means neat animals, horses, sheep, goats, swine, mules, and asses.
“Neglect” The inability or unwellness of an owner to provide the animal with shelter, food, water, and medical
care if that inability or unwillingness causes substantial risk of harm to the animal’s health or welfare.
"Owner" means any person having temporary or permanent custody of, sheltering, or having charge of,
harboring, exercising control over, or having property rights to, any animal covered by this ordinance, for more
than six consecutive days pursuant to ARS §11-1001.
“Predatory Animal” means animals of the genera canis (e.g. Mexican Wolf), Ursus (e.g. Grizzly Bear) and Felis
(e.g. Jaguar), excluding domestic feral cats that have been spayed and returned to the wild.
“Release” means the act of setting free to roam at large.
"Stray dog" means any dog at large that is not wearing a valid license tag pursuant to Section 2.1 hereof.
"Suitable enclosure" means a vehicle, cage, kennel, fenced yard, or other area which effectively prevents the
dog from escaping.
"Veterinarian" means any veterinarian licensed to practice in Arizona or employed in Arizona by a
governmental agency.
"Vicious dog" means:
 any dog with a known propensity, tendency, or disposition to attack unprovoked, to cause injury or to
otherwise endanger the safety of human beings or domestic animals; or
 any dog that attacks a human being or a domestic animal without provocation; or
 any dog owned, harbored, or trained for the purpose of dog fighting; or
 any dog that has been so declared after a hearing before a justice of the peace or a municipal magistrate.
“Wild Animal” means any live monkey, nonhuman primate, raccoon, skunk, fox, leopard, panther, tiger, lion,
lynx, or any other warm-blooded animal that can normally be found in the wild.  The term “wild animal”
includes feral dogs and cats, but does not include domestic, tame dogs (canis familiaris, but excludes feral dogs
or hybrids with wolfs, coyotes, or jackals), domestic cats (felis domesticus, but excluding feral cats ot hygrids
with ocelots or margays), farm animals, rodents, any hybrid animal that is part wild, and captive-bred species of
common cage birds.
1.9 Appointment of County Enforcement Agent
Pursuant to A.R.S § 11-1005, the Director of the Navajo County Public Health Services District is hereby
appointed as the County Enforcement Agent for purposes of enforcing this Ordinance, the State Animal Control

<!-- page 107 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 4
Statutes, and any municipal ordinance(s) which the Board may contract to enforce pursuant to A.R.S § 11-1005.
The Director may appoint employees of the District as Deputy County Enforcement Agents with full authority
to act on behalf of the County Enforcement Agent. All references herein to the County Enforcement Agent shall
also include Deputy County Enforcement Agents.
1.10 Enforcement
This Ordinance may be enforced by the County Enforcement Agent or any peace officer. The County
Enforcement Agent is hereby authorized to enter onto private property in the performance of the Agent’s duties
under this Ordinance. It shall be unlawful for any person to interfere with the County Enforcement Agent in the
performance of the Agent’s duties.
1.11 Keeping of Wild or Exotic Animals
It shall be unlawful for anyone to own, possess, harbor, or permit at large any wild, exotic, or feral animal
without the prior, written permission of the County Enforcement Agent, and other applicable federal or State
agencies. Such permission shall be given only if it is demonstrated to the satisfaction of the County
Enforcement Agent that the animal will not constitute a threat to public health or safety. Experts who are
familiar with the species and the particular animal shall prepare and file reports satisfactory to the county that
such animal will not present a danger. Further, neighbors within three hundred (300) feet shall be notified in
writing by the animal owner (with copies of such notices to the county) about the keeping of any such wild,
exotic, or feral animal.
1.12 Keeping of Horses
Horses. Owners of horses and other animals of the equine family shall comply with the requirements set forth in
Section 302, "Use Regulations”, 15- The keeping of horses, as amended of the County Zoning Ordinance.
1.13 Predatory Animals
It shall be unlawful for any person to import within the boundaries of Navajo County any predatory animal for
the purpose of releasing such animal into the wild, knowing that such animal will be released into the wild by
another. It shall be unlawful for any person to release into the wild or assist another in releasing into the wild
any predatory animal within the boundaries of Navajo County. The United States Fish and Wildlife Services has
reintroduced predatory animals within the Blue Range Wolf Recovery Area, which consists of the entire
Apache and Gila National Forests with a portion in Navajo County. 
Any individual may kill, injure, or harass a predatory animal when and to the degree a reasonable person would
believe that deadly force is immediately necessary to protect himself or another person against a predatory
animal because the predatory animal poses an imminent threat of severe bodily harm to any individual.
1.14 Remedial Actions to Avoid Contact between Humans and Certain Dangerous Species
It shall be unlawful for any person to fail to take remedial action to avoid contact between humans and bears,
coyotes, or javelinas after having been notified by the Code Enforcement Agent, an officer of the Arizona Game
and Fish Department, or any peace officer that bears, coyotes or javelinas are in the area and that remedial
actions should be taken. Such notice may include, without limitation, actions to secure or remove outdoor trash,
cooking grills, pet food, bird feeders, and any other food source or attractant likely to attract bears, coyotes, or
javelinas. 
Furthermore, it is the personal responsibility of every property owner or occupant to take immediate remedial
action after any initial contact or conflict with a bear, coyote, or javelina on their property. This includes
securing or removing outdoor trash, cooking grills, pet food, bird feeders, and any other food source or
attractant that could potentially lure bears, coyotes, or javelinas. This obligation continues for the period
prescribed by the official giving notice or for a minimum of 30 days after the initial contact or conflict.

<!-- page 108 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 5
Article 2 – Provisions Applicable to Dogs
2.1 Requirement for Dogs to be Licensed and Vaccinated
Every dog three months of age or over that is kept, harbored, or maintained in the unincorporated area of
Navajo County for at least 30 consecutive days of each calendar year shall be licensed except as provided in
Section 2.2 hereof. License fees and penalties shall be set forth on a separate schedule of fees adopted by the
Board, subject to amendment from time to time. The schedule may provide for lower fees for dogs which have
been surgically altered to be incapable of procreation.
Before a license is issued for any dog, the owner or a veterinarian shall present a paper or electronic or facsimile
copy of a current anti-rabies vaccination certificate signed by a veterinarian stating the owner's name and
address and giving the dog's description, date of vaccination, manufacturer and serial number of the vaccine
used, and date revaccination is due. A duplicate of each rabies vaccination certificate issued shall be transmitted
to the County Enforcement Agent within two weeks of the date the dog was vaccinated. A dog vaccinated in
another state may be licensed if, at the time of licensing, the owner presents a current vaccination certificate
signed by a veterinarian licensed to practice in the other state or employed by a governmental agency in the
other state, stating the owner's name and address and giving the dog's description, date of vaccination and type,
and manufacturer and serial number of the vaccine used. The County Enforcement Agent may schedule
vaccination clinics as deemed necessary. All vaccinations shall be performed by a veterinarian.
Each dog licensed under the terms of this Ordinance shall receive, at the time of licensing, a durable tag on
which shall be inscribed the name of the County, the number of the license, and the year in which it expires.
The tag shall be attached to a collar or harness which shall be worn by the dog at all times.
It shall be unlawful for any person to fail, within 15 days after written notice from the County Enforcement
Agent, to obtain a license for a dog required to be licensed; to counterfeit an official dog tag.  To remove such a
tag from any dog for the purpose of intentional and malicious mischief; or to place a dog tag upon a dog unless
the tag was issued for that particular dog.
2.1.1 Wolf-Hybrids
Although wolf-hybrids are defined as dogs for purposes of this Ordinance, it has not been scientifically
established that rabies vaccine is effective in wolf-hybrids. See the Arizona Department of Health Services’
“Manual for Rabies Control and Bite Management.” Therefore, a wolf-hybrid which bites a person or
subjects a person to its saliva shall be deemed a wild animal for purposes of A.R.S §11-1014 and shall be
subject to immediate destruction for rabies testing even if it has been vaccinated and licensed in accordance
with this Ordinance.
2.2 Kennel Permits
2.2.1 Definitions:
In this Section 2.2
"Kennel" shall mean an enclosed, controlled area, inaccessible to other animals, in which a person keeps,
harbors, or maintains five or more dogs under controlled conditions.
Unless each dog is individually licensed pursuant to Section 2.1 hereof, a person operating a kennel shall obtain
a kennel permit issued by the County Enforcement Agent on behalf of the Board. Permit fees and penalties shall
be set forth on a separate schedule of fees adopted by the Board, subject to amendment from time to time.
A dog remaining within the kennel is not required to be individually licensed under Section 2.1 hereof. A dog
leaving the controlled kennel conditions shall be licensed under Section 2.1 unless the dog is merely being
transported to another permitted kennel or for purposes of medical treatment or sponsored exhibition.
The county shall deny a kennel permit to any person who has been convicted of a violation of ARS §13-2910 or

<!-- page 109 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 6
§13-2910.01 or any other state, county, or municipal animal welfare law, except violations of license and leash
laws (ARS §11-1009E).
It shall be unlawful for any person to fail to obtain a Kennel Permit within 30 days after written notice from the
County Enforcement Agent, unless each dog is individually licensed within said 30-day period; A.R.S §11-
1009G.
2.3 Prohibition Against Dog(s) at Large
It shall be unlawful for an owner to cause or allow, either willfully or through a failure to exercise reasonable
control, the dog to be at large; A.R.S §11-1012C. Provided, however, that a dog may be at large while engaging
in or being trained for legal hunting activities or herding livestock, being exhibited, or trained at a kennel club
event, assisting a peace officer or military officer, or assisting a blind, deaf, or disabled person, so long as the
dog is under direct and effective voice control.
Injury to any person or damage to any property by a dog while at large shall be the full responsibility of the dog
owner or person or persons responsible for the dog when such damages were inflicted; A.R.S. §11-1020.
2.4 Impoundment of Stray Dogs and Cats
Any stray dog or cat may be impounded by the County Enforcement Agent or a peace officer. If in the
judgment of the Agent or officer any dog at large is dangerous or fierce and a threat to human safety and cannot
be safely impounded, it may be put to death.
The Board may provide or authorize a County Pound or enter into a cooperative agreement with a municipality,
a veterinarian, animal shelter, or an Arizona incorporated humane society for the establishment and operation of
a County Pound; A.R.S. §11-1013.
Pound fees for impounding and maintaining dogs and cats at the County Pound shall be set forth on a separate
schedule of fees adopted by the Board, subject to amendment from time to time.
Each impounded dog or cat shall be kept and maintained at the County Pound for a minimum of 72 hours unless
sooner claimed by the owner. The owner may reclaim the impounded dog or cat upon furnishing proof of
ownership, paying the applicable pound fees, and complying with the licensing and vaccination requirements of
this Ordinance; A.R.S. §11-1013F. Any other person may purchase the impounded dog or cat upon expiration
of the impoundment period, provided such person pays the applicable pound fees pays and complies with the
licensing and vaccination requirements of this Ordinance.
If the impounded dog or cat is not reclaimed or purchased within a reasonable time after the expiration of the
impoundment period, the County Enforcement Agent may cause the dog or cat to be disposed of in a humane
manner; A.R.S. §11-1013G. The Agent may destroy impounded sick or injured dogs or cats at any time if
destruction is necessary to prevent the dog or cat from suffering or to prevent the spread of disease.
2.5 Prohibition Against Excessive or Unrestrained Barking
It shall be unlawful for an owner to allow, either willfully or through a failure to exercise reasonable control, the
dog to bark or howl in an unreasonable, continual, or unprovoked manner which disturbs the peace and quiet of
any person; A.R.S. §11-1005A.5.
2.6 Aggressive Dog(s) Fighting
It shall be unlawful for any person to own or possess any dog for the purpose of dog fighting or to train,
torment, badger, bait or use any dog for the purpose of dog fighting or for the purpose of causing or
encouraging the dog to engage in unprovoked attacks upon human beings or domestic animals; Criminal A.R.S.
§13-2910.01.
It shall be unlawful for an owner of an aggressive dog to allow, either willfully or through a failure to exercise

<!-- page 110 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 7
reasonable control, the dog to go unconfined. An aggressive dog is "confined" for purposes of this Section 2.6
only if it is kept indoors or in a securely enclosed and locked pen or dog run or is muzzled and securely
restrained by a leash, cord, rope, chain, or other physical restraint not more than six feet in length and of
sufficient strength to control the dog.
2.7 Quarantine Animals
 In a rabies quarantine, no dogs shall be permitted at large. Each dog shall be confined within an
enclosure on the owner's property not able to be around other animals, make sure the dog is secured so
that it is confined entirely to the owner's property, or on a leash not to exceed six feet in length and
directly under the owner's control when not on the owner's property A.R.S §11-1012B.
 An unvaccinated dog or cat that bites any person shall be confined and quarantined in the County’s
animal shelter, a County pound or, on request of and at the expense of the owner, at a veterinary hospital
for a period of not less than ten (10) days. The quarantine period shall start on the day of the bite
incident. If the day of the bite is not known, the quarantine period shall start on the first day of
impoundment.
 A dog properly vaccinated pursuant to this ordinance that bites any person may be confined and
quarantined at the home of the owner or wherever the dog is harbored.
 The owner of a dog which bites a person when the person is in or on a public place or lawfully in or on a
private place, including the property of the owner of the dog, is liable for damages suffered by the
person bitten, regardless of the former viciousness of the dog or the owner's knowledge of its
viciousness; A.R.S §11-1025A.
Article 3 – Provisions Applicable to All Animals (Including Dogs)
3.1 Prohibition Against Cruelty to, or Neglect of, Animals
3.1.1 Cruelty
It is unlawful for any person to knowingly, intentionally, or recklessly overdrive, overload, overwork,
torture, torment, cruelly beat, inflict unnecessary cruelty or suffering upon, cruelly abandon or neglect,
carry in or upon a vehicle in an unnecessarily cruel or inhumane manner, mutilate or unlawfully kill an
animal, or to cause or procure an animal to be so treated; A.R.S. §13-2910.
3.1.2 Neglect
The purpose of this Subsection 3.1.2 is to ensure that animals under human custody or control are
housed in healthy environments and are provided with proper food, water, shelter, medical care, exercise
space and ventilation. Any person owning an animal shall ensure:
A.  That the animal receives daily food which is free from contamination and is of sufficient quantity
and nutritive value to maintain the animal in good health.
B. That potable water is accessible to the animal at all times, either free-flowing or in a clean
receptacle.
C. That, except for livestock, all animals have convenient access to natural or artificial shelter
throughout the year. Any such artificial shelter shall be structurally sound and maintained in
good repair to protect the animal from injury and from the elements, and of sufficient size to
permit the animal to enter, stand, turn around and lie down in a natural manner. Any shelter
which does not protect the animal from temperature extremes or precipitation, or which does not

<!-- page 111 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 8
provide adequate ventilation or drainage, shall not comply with this section. Any shelter, all
bedding, and any spaces accessible to the animal shall be maintained in a manner which
minimizes the risk of the animal contracting disease, being injured, or becoming infested with
parasites.
D. That the animal receives care and medical treatment for debilitating injuries, parasites, and
diseases, sufficient to maintain the animal in good health and minimize suffering.
E. That the animal is given adequate exercise space within an enclosure that shall be constructed of
material, and in a manner, to minimize the risk of injury to the animal, and shall encompass
sufficient usable space to keep the animal in good condition. With the exception of temporary
tethering of horses, the use of tie-outs such as chains, leashes, wires, cables, ropes, or similar
restraining devices for the purpose of animal confinement is hereby prohibited.
F. That the animal has access to adequate ventilation and is protected from temperature extremes at
all times. In this connection, it is unlawful for any person to keep any animal in a vehicle or other
enclosed space in which the temperature is either so high or so low, or the ventilation is so
inadequate, as to endanger the animal's life or health. The County Enforcement Agent or any
peace officer is authorized to use whatever force is reasonable and necessary to remove any
animal from a vehicle or other enclosed space whenever it appears that the animal's life or health
is endangered by extreme temperatures or lack of ventilation within the vehicle or other enclosed
space. Neither the Agent nor any peace officer shall be liable for damages to property caused by
the use of reasonable force to remove an animal from such a vehicle or other enclosed space
under such circumstances.
It is unlawful for any owner to knowingly, intentionally, or recklessly neglect an animal. Any provision
of this Subsection 3.1.2 may be waived as dictated by treatment under the direction of a licensed
veterinarian.
3.2 Aggressive or Destructive Animals
3.2.1 Definitions
In this Section 3.2:
"Bite" means any penetration of the skin by the teeth of any animal.
"Destructive animal" means any animal that has a propensity, without provocation, to destroy, damage,
or cause damage to the property of a person other than the animal's owner.
"Aggressive dog" means any dog that has bitten a person or domestic animal without provocation or that
has a known history of attacking persons or domestic animals without provocation. The animal has a
propensity, without provocation, to bite, attempt to bite, endanger, or otherwise injure or cause to be
injured a person or other animals, or one that has been declared aggressive by a court of competent
jurisdiction.
"Provocation" means any behavior toward an animal or its owner which is likely to cause a defensive
reaction by the animal.
3.2.2 Prohibition Against Aggressive and Destructive Animals
It is unlawful for any person to keep, control, harbor or otherwise have under control any animal which
is aggressive or destructive. This prohibition shall not apply to zoos, wild animal parks or animal
shelters, to law enforcement agencies or to persons who are in compliance with an order of a court of

<!-- page 112 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 9
competent jurisdiction.
A person who owns or who is responsible for the care of an aggressive dog shall take responsible care to
prohibit the dog from escaping to the outside of the residence or an enclosed are, yard or structure.  Will
control the dog in a manner that prevents the dog from biting or attacking a person or domestic animal at
all times while the dog is off the owner’s or responsible party’s property; A.R.S. §11-1014.01A.2.
3.3 Removal and Impoundment of Aggressive or Destructive Animals, or Animals Subject to Cruelty or
Neglect
3.3.1 Authority
The County Enforcement Agent or any peace officer is hereby authorized and empowered to remove
and impound any animal in plain view, or pursuant to a valid search warrant, if the Agent or officer has
probable cause to believe any of the following:
 That an animal is in distress caused by cruelty or neglect, including without limitation mistreatment,
tie out, exposure to the elements, extremes of temperature, lack of adequate ventilation or drainage,
lack of sanitation, deprivation of proper food or potable water, restraint, restriction of movement,
confinement, lack of sufficient exercise space, constrictive gear, injury, illness, physical impairment,
or parasites.
 That an animal is aggressive or destructive or may otherwise be a danger to the safety of any person
or other animal.
3.3.2 Procedure
The following procedures shall be followed by the County Enforcement Agent or a peace officer when
any animal is removed or impounded pursuant to Subsection 3.3.1. Refer to Navajo County Hearing
Officer Rules of Procedure-Resolution No. 50-09 for further information about Hearing Officer
hearings:
 If the owner is known, and unless the owner signs a statement permanently relinquishing ownership
of the animal to the County Pound, the owner shall be provided with a written notice of the County's
intent to file, within ten days of receipt of the notice, a written request with the local Justice of the
Peace for a hearing to determine if the animal should be returned to the owner or forfeited to the
County Pound. If the owner's whereabouts cannot be determined, notice shall be mailed to the
owner's last-known address by registered or certified mail, return receipt requested.
 Upon seizure pursuant to Subsection 3.3.1, the owner must post 15 days of impoundment fees in
advance as a bond to defray some of the costs of boarding, impoundment, and any required
veterinary care. This sum shall be listed on the notice provided to the owner pursuant to the
preceding paragraph. If the bond is not posted within ten days of the notice, the animal shall be
deemed forfeited to the County Pound to be placed by adoption in a suitable home or humanely
destroyed.
 If the county files a written request for a hearing before the Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer;
A.R.S. §11-1006, the hearing shall be set for a date not less than ten nor more than 15 working days
after the request has been filed. The county enforcement agent shall use a uniform traffic ticket and
complaint for civil traffic cases pursuant to the rules of procedure in traffic cases adopted by the
supreme court, modified as applicable, in citing persons for violations of ordinances adopted with a
civil penalty pursuant to section 11-1005, subsection A, paragraph 6, subdivision (b), not to exceed
five hundred dollars for each violation.

<!-- page 113 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 10
 If the owner fails to appear at the hearing, the Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer shall order the
animal forfeited to the County Pound to be placed by adoption in a suitable home or humanely
destroyed. At the hearing in front of a Hearing Officer if the “Owner” is considered “Default” -fails
to appear, the Hearing Officer will enter Judgement for the County Enforcement Agency.  If neither
the “Owner” nor the “Owner’s Attorney” appears at the Hearing after having denied responsibility in
writing, the hearing shall proceed in the absence of the “Owner”.  If the Hearing Officer is satisfied
that the County Enforcement Agency has proven the violation alleged in the citation, the Hearing
Officer shall enter Judgment for the County Enforcement Agency.
 At the hearing, the issue to be decided by the Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer shall be made
by a preponderance of the evidence:
The animal is in distress caused by cruelty or neglect, including without limitation mistreatment,
tie out, exposure to the elements, extremes of temperature, lack of adequate ventilation or
drainage, lack of sanitation, deprivation of proper food or potable water, restraint, restriction of
movement, confinement, lack of sufficient exercise space, constrictive gear, injury, illness,
physical impairment, or parasites; or
The animal is aggressive or destructive or may otherwise be a danger to the safety of any person
or other animal.
 If the “Owner” testifies at a hearing held pursuant to this subsection, the “Owner” does not, by so
testifying, waive the right to remain silent during any criminal trial related to the animal or its
treatment. If the “Owner” does testify, neither this fact nor the substance of the testimony shall be
mentioned at any criminal trial unless the owner testifies at the trial concerning the same matter(s).
 Upon finding that the animal is in distress caused by cruelty or neglect or is aggressive, destructive
or otherwise a danger, the Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer may, in addition to whatever
penalties may be imposed pursuant to this Ordinance or state statute, impose upon the “Owner” such
conditions and restrictions as will assure the proper and humane treatment of the animal and the
safety of the public; order the animal forfeited to the County Pound or other agency for adoption in a
suitable home or humane destruction; or make such other orders as the Justice of the Peace or
Hearing Officer deems appropriate under the circumstances.
 Appeal of the decision of the Justice of the Peace shall be by way of a special action to the Superior
Court on the record of the hearing. If appeal is made for the Hearing Officer both the “Owner” and
the County Enforcement Agency has the right to appeal a Judgement.  In order to appeal, a written
notice of appeal must be filled with the Clerk of the Board no later than 15 days after Judgement was
mailed.  If either party claims the record to be incomplete or lost, and the Justice of the Peace or
Hearing Officer who conducted the hearing so certifies, a new hearing shall be conducted before that
Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer. If the Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer has ordered the
animal to be forfeited, the “Owner” must post a bond equivalent to 60 days of impoundment costs in
order to perfect the appeal. Notice of the amount due shall be given to the “Owner” by the Justice of
the Peace or Hearing officer at the time of the hearing.
Unless good cause is shown, the owner shall be liable for all boarding, impoundment and veterinary fees
arising out of the animal's impoundment until a final decision by the Justice of the Peace or Hearing
Officer (including the pendency of an appeal). No Justice of the Peace or Hearing Officer may waive or
reduce any fees arising out of the animal's impoundment unless the “Owner” prevails, or the County
Enforcement Agency voluntary dismisses the violation in writing the on or before the date of the
hearing.

<!-- page 114 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 11
3.3.3 Non-Exclusivity
Nothing in Section 3.3 shall be construed as precluding or requiring the use of any other remedies, civil
or criminal. The remedies provided herein are not punitive and are cumulative with and not exclusive of
any other remedies provided for by law. Nothing in Section 3.3 shall be construed to estop the
prosecution of the owner for violations of this Ordinance or state statutes.
3.4 Excessive Noise
3.4.1 Definition
In this Section 3.4
“Property line" means the line which represents the legal limits of property (including an apartment,
condominium, room, or other dwelling unit) owned, leased, or otherwise occupied by a person, business,
corporation, or institution. In cases involving sound from an activity on a public street or other public
right-of-way, the "property line" shall be the nearest boundary of the public right-of-way.
3.4.2 Prohibition
It is unlawful for any person to own, possess, keep, or control any animal which frequently or for
continuous duration howls, barks, meows, squawks or makes other sounds which are clearly audible
beyond the property line of the property on which the animal is located, and which disturb the public
peace, quiet or comfort of the neighbors or other persons.
3.5 Rabies Quarantine Area
The Board may from time to time declare a rabies quarantine area on the recommendation of the Director of the
Navajo County Public Health Services District due to the occurrence of rabies in animals in or adjacent to the
area. If a rabies quarantine area is declared, the Board and the County Enforcement Agent shall institute an
emergency program for the control of rabies within the area, provided that any regulations restricting or
involving the movement of livestock within the area shall be developed by the State Veterinarian.
Article 4 – Penalties and Enforcement
A violation of this Ordinance can be classified up to a Class 2 Misdemeanor pursuant to Arizona Revised
Statutes or heard before a Hearing Officer pursuant to A.R.S §11-1006 and Resolution 50-09, “A Resolution of
the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, Adopting the Navajo County Hearing Officer Rules of Procedure”.
Each day that a violation continues shall be deemed a separate offense.
The provisions of this Ordinance are supplementary to the State Animal Control Statutes; A.R.S §13-1208
(concerning vicious animals); §13-2910 (prohibiting cruelty to animals); §13-2910.01 (prohibiting dog
fighting); and all other provisions of the Arizona Revised Statutes which may provide separate criminal or civil
penalties and fees for conduct that is within the scope of this Ordinance.
The County Enforcement Agent is authorized to issue citations pursuant to A.R.S §11-1007 for a violation of
this Ordinance, the State Animal Control Statutes, and any municipal ordinance which the Board of Supervisors
may contract to enforce. The procedure for the issuance of notices to appear shall be as provided for peace
officers in A.R.S §13-3903, except that the County Enforcement Agent shall not make an arrest before issuing
the notice. The issuance of citations pursuant to this Ordinance shall be subject to the provisions of A.R.S §13-
3899.
This Ordinance may also be enforced by any peace officer.
Pursuant to A.R.S. §11-251.08, the board of supervisors may adopt fee schedules for any specific products and
services the county provides to the public.  The attached fee schedule within this ordinance shall be approved

<!-- page 115 -->
Navajo County Animal Care & Control                                                                                                 12
and amended when needed and the board of supervisors may adopt an additional charge or separate individual
charge therein.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: Agreement for purchase of Right-of-Way
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Donald Perkins, Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: William Flake
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Land Purchase Agreement for additional Right-of-Way along Bourdon Ranch Road
for drainage purposes.
Background:
Bourdon Ranch Road at Millett Swale has experienced unsafe stormwater runoff events due to the failure
of Millett Swale Dam.  The County is working on a Capital Improvement Project to increase the size and
carrying capacity of two existing culverts conveying flows under Bourdon Ranch Road.  The County has
all the necessary easements to move forward with the construction project, which is expected to start at
the beginning of August. This property owner has chosen to deed the additional Right-of-Way to Navajo
County for drainage purposes.
The signing of the agreement will grant Navajo County title over the property for additional
Right-of-Way for drainage purposes for the maintenance of the re-constructed culvert at the Millett Swale
drainage for Bourdon Ranch Road.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the approval of signing the Land Purchase Agreement granting Navajo County
additional Right-of-Way on Bourdon Ranch Road.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. b.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: Winter Parking Ordinance
Submitted By: Cody Cooper, Planning Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Presented By: Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Ordinance Number 02-2024, approving the
removal of motor vehicles, trailers, and storage containers from County-maintained rights-of-way during
snow season and for the enforcement of parking restrictions by issuance of citations and/or towing.
Background:
During the winter season, it is imperative that Public Works Department Staff is able to respond to
weather that causes accumulations of snow and ice to mitigate difficult driving conditions until the
weather allows driving conditions to return to normal.  Vehicles parked within County-maintained
rights-of-way during the winter season can cause unreasonable delay and/or limit the ability of Public
Works Staff to complete this task.  These delays and limitations on response present an impact to the
safety of the residents of Navajo County.
Due to the potential difficulties caused by those that park or store items within maintained rights-of-way,
and the volatile nature of weather, the staff is recommending that there be an Ordinance that restricts
parking in County-maintained rights-of-way from November 1st until April 1st of every year.  Contained
within this Ordinance are provisions for enforcement of these restrictions, including issuance of citations
and/or towing of motor vehicles, trailers, and storage containers for those that would violate the outlined
provisions.
Regarding enforcement, there would be the following provisions: 
First offense: A fine of not less than $100.00, in addition to any other penalties that may be
imposed for such an offense.
Subsequent offenses: A fine of not less than $200.00, in addition to any other penalties that may be
imposed for such an offense.
Any offense: Potential for towing and storing of motor vehicle, trailer, and/or storage container, at
the owner's expense.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of this Ordinance.

ORDINANCE ___-2024
SNOW PARKING ORDINANCE
An Ordinance of the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County, Arizona, to provide for the
removal of motor vehicles, trailers, and storage containers from County-maintained rights-of-
way during snow season and for the enforcement of parking restrictions by issuance of citations
and/or towing.
WHEREAS, concerning County-maintained rights-of-way, the Navajo County Board of
Supervisors, pursuant to A.R.S. § 11-251(4), may lay out, maintain, control, and manage public
roads within the County; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, pursuant to A.R.S. § 11-251.05(1), may
adopt all ordinances necessary or proper to carry out the duties, responsibilities and functions of
the county which are not otherwise specifically limited by section 11-251 or any other law or in
conflict with any rule or law of Arizona; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Public Works Department considers that the forecast of certain
difficult driving conditions caused by snow and or ice warrant immediate response and work by
its roadway maintenance forces to mitigate these driving conditions until weather allows driving
conditions to return to normal; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, after considering the testimony and other
evidence presented at the public hearing, finds that the “Snow Parking Ordinance” is consistent
with the public health, safety and general welfare and should be adopted as set forth herein.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors as
follows:
1. Each year, between November 1 st and April 1 st, inclusive, all vehicles, trailers, and
storage containers shall be kept clear at all times from all County-maintained rights-of-
way.  No vehicle, trailer, or storage container shall be parked in a manner that restricts
traffic/road maintenance, including snow plowing.  Any vehicle, trailer, and/or storage
container parked in a County-maintained right-of-way that is restricting traffic/road
maintenance during the prohibited period stated herein or is otherwise parked in a manner
that creates a threat to the health, safety, or welfare of the County, may be cited and may
be towed away and stored at the owner’s expense by a law enforcement officer.

<!-- page 156 -->
2. Persons Liable: Whenever any vehicle is parked in violation of any of the provisions of
any ordinance prohibiting or restricting parking, the person in whose name such vehicle
is registered shall be responsible for such violation and is subject to the penalties therefor.
3. Defense to Liability: Subsection two (2) of this section shall not prevent a person from
presenting evidence in any prosecution of a parking violation that a vehicle was not
illegally parked or that said vehicle was not registered to said person at the time of the
alleged offense.  Proof that a person other than the registered owner was operating the
vehicle at the time of the violation shall not constitute a valid defense to the substantive
offense.
4. In County-maintained rights-of-way, violation of this Ordinance shall be a civil traffic
offense.  However, in addition to any other penalties that may be imposed for such an
offense, the amount of the fine for violation of this Ordinance shall not be less than
$100.00 for a first offense and shall not be less than $200.00 for any subsequent offenses.
5. Definitions: Vehicle, Motor Vehicle, and Trailer shall have the same meaning as defined
in A.R.S. § 28-101.  Storage Container shall have the same meaning as “Portable storage
container” meaning a portable, weather-resistant, commercially leased or rented
receptacle designed and used for the storage or shipment of personal property, building
materials, or merchandise.
SECTION 2. Severability.
If any section, subsection, sentence, clause, phrase or portion of this ordinance or any part
of the code adopted herein by reference is for any reason held to be invalid or
unconstitutional by the decision of any court of competent jurisdiction, such decision shall
not affect the validity of the remaining portions thereof.
SECTION 3. Clerical Corrections
The County Clerk is hereby authorized to correct typographical and grammatical errors, as
well as errors of wording and punctuation, as necessary related to this ordinance as
amended herein, and to make formatting changes needed for purposes of clarity and form,
or consistency, within thirty (30) days following adoption by the Board of Supervisors.
SECTION 4. Effective Date
This ordinance shall become effective thirty (30) days following adoption by the Board of
Supervisors.
PASSED AND ADOPTED this ____ day of ____________, 2024 by the Navajo County Board
of Supervisors.

Title: ZC 24-001: RIM COUNTRY RV PARK, DISTRICT V
Submitted By: Tyler Richards, Planner I
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Presented By: Tyler Richards
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 22-2024, approving/denying a
Zone Change from R-2 to Commercial-Residential (C-R) on an approximately 0.33-acre portion of an
approximately 2.28-acre parcel, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive. 
Background:
APN#: 212-18-035A, T09N, R22E, S22 of the GSRM. Owner: Rim Country RVP
LLC. Agent: Gammage and Burnham.
Currently, there is an existing, 38-space RV park occupying APNs #212-43-001, and 212-18-078C. This
is currently a legal, non-conforming use of the parcel, and the applicant seeks to bring those parcels into
compliance, in order to expand the RV Park to APN #212-18-035A.
The proposed project will have the following: 
Additional 28 new RV lots, for a total of 66 RV spaces.
6ft. solid fencing surrounding the property.
Improved emergency access along Rainbow Lake Drive.
Extended gravel driveway for access to the 28 new lots. 
Open, undeveloped areas. 
Recommendation: 
On May 16th, 2024, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to
recommend approval of the Zone Change. Additionally, Planning and Development Services Staff
recommend approval of the Zone Change.

Re: Zone Change Letter of Explanation

Navajo County Assessor Parcel No. 212-18-035A 
 
 
Dear Navajo County Planning & Development Department: 
 
 We represent Rim Country RVP, LLC  (the “Owner”) regarding the three properties located at 
3754 Rainbow Lake Drive, 3796 Rainbow Lake Drive, and 3629 State Route 260 . The current primary 
use of the three properties is a 38-unit mobile home park, known as the Rim Country RV Park (the “Park”). 
On behalf of the Park, we are submitting this request to rezone a portion of the Park from R-2 to C-R with 
a Special Use Permit (“SUP”) to allow for expansion of the Park . See attached Exhibit ‘A’: Aerial Map 
with Rezone Area Highlighted . An additional application will be submitted to request the Special Use  
Permit for the RV Park use on all three properties. 
 
Current Conditions 
 
  The Park is approximately 4.76 acres. Most of the Site is currently zoned Commercial-Residential 
(C-R), with a small , triangular shaped  portion zoned Multiple-Family Residential (R-2). See attached 
Exhibit ‘B’: Current Zoning Map. As shown on the attached Aerial Map, the majority of the 38 total RV 
lots are located on two of the three properties within the park. The third property located at 3796 Rainbow 
Lake Drive, also known as Navajo County Assessor Parcel Number 212 -18-035A (the “Site”) , only 
accommodates a small portion of the 38 total RV lots . Most of the Site area is currently being used as 
open space with one small community clubhouse building. This Site is where the proposed Park expansion 
will take place. 
 
Project Proposal 
 
The proposed expansion of the existing RV Park  is intended to increase the number of RV lots 
provided. This expansion will only affect the previously mentioned Site located at 3796 Rainbow Lake 
Drive. The other two properties that make up the full Park will remain mostly unchanged. The proposed 
expansion will result in approximately 28 new RV lots. The northwest corner of the expansion area will 
TELEPHONE (602) 256-0566

12705.2.3390576.2 
be reserved for a detention basin and the southeast corner will be preserved as open space with the existing 
building to remain. See attached Exhibit ‘C’: Site Plan. 
 
 As shown in the Site Plan, the existing drive located on the western side of the existing Park will 
be extended to provide access to the 28 new lots. This existing drive provides access to  both Rainbow 
Lake Drive to the west  and State Route 260  to the east  with vehicle gates at each driveway. There is 
currently no plan for there to be direct access to Rainbow Drive from the Site itself.  
 
Zone Change Request 
 
 In order to accommodate the expansion of the RV Park, we are requesting a zone change from R-
2 to C-R for the small triangular portion of the Site shown in the attached Exhibit ‘B’: Current Zoning 
Map. There are no County records available that explain the nature or purpose of this odd triangular zoning 
area within a larger parcel of land. Rezoning to C-R would correct this anomaly and make all three parcels 
share one zoning designation.  
 
This zone change request will be accompanied with an additional request for a Special Use Permit 
to allow for the RV Park use in the C-R zoning district on all three properties located within the Park. As 
currently zoned under the R-2 district, the Site does not permit RV Parks with a SUP, making it necessary 
to rezone to C-R in order to obtain a SUP for the Site. 
 
 With a zone change to C-R with a SUP, the Park will be able to expand further into the currently 
unused, open space area on the western portion of the Site. This expansion would fit in with the existing 
developments in the surrounding area. As shown in the attached ‘Exhibit D’: Surrounding Uses Map, the 
Park is surrounded mostly by single-family detached residences and multiple other RV Parks including: 
 
- Rainbow Forest RV Park adjacent to the southern border of the Park; 
- Running Bear Mobile Resort southeast of the site off of Running Bear Rd and State Route 260; 
- Rainbow Lake Mobile Home Park south of the Park on Rainbow Lake Dr and 2nd Street; and, 
- Pete’s Retreat RV Park just south of the Rainbow Lake Mobile Home Park. 
Given the context of the surrounding area, the expansion of the existing RV Park use would be a very 
consistent use for the Site and surrounding community.  
 
Schedule for Development 
 
 Development of the additional RV lots would occur promptly after approval.

Taylor A. Manemann
From: Jeanette Williams
Sent: Tuesday, March 21, 2023 11:44 AM
To: Stephen W. Anderson
Cc: Taylor A. Manemann
Subject: Will-Serve Letter - Frontier Communications re RIM Country RV Park
Stephen, 
 
Pursuant to our earlier discussion, I called Frontier Communications and spoke to “Jill” (based in 
California).  I asked for contact information/email address of the person responsible for will-serve 
letters.   
 
Jill informed me that Frontier Communications does not have a department/or person for that and 
they do not send will serve letters, or even email confirmations of serviceability. 
 
What she would tell me verbally was that, as of the current date, phone service is available with no 
issues.  As far as Internet service, there are no issues, and they have available ports.  However, as the 
ports fill up, there might not be any availability when we need it.  Internet service consists of 6 
megabytes per second – DSL service.

March 27, 2023 
Re: Rim Country RV Park 
s NNITARIIMTRIC  
To Whom It May Concern, 
We have been asked to provide a "Letter of Serviceability"  concerning parcels 212-18-035A, 212-18-
078C and 212-43-001. 
It is our understanding that the three parcels listed above are currently  served by sewer through a 
private lift station located on the parcel listed as 212-43-001. 
If the desire for the property owners is to change  the current connection location we would need more 
information as to the fact that sewer is only available along Highway 260. Any change would require the 
property owner(s) to extend the sewer line to the desired location at their cost since this would only 
benefit the property owner(s). 
It has also been brought to our understanding  that there is a planned expansion of this RV Park (55% 
increase), which will also increase the capacity fees for this property to be paid by the owner(s) before 
additional loading is added to our system. We cannot quote a price for the additional  charges until we 
review the plans that show the planned expansion. 
In summary, the District does currently provide serviceability  for the Rim Country RV Park, but any 
changes will need to be reviewed  before final approval can be issued. 
If there are any other questions, please let me know. 
Sincerely, 
2

Rezone and Special Use Permit Applications  
Neighborhood Meeting Summary 
 
I. Details and Techniques Used to Involve the Public 
 
Affected Parties  
 
A contact list was provided by the Navajo County Planning and Development Services 
Department for property owners within 300 feet of the project site. The County did not 
identify any additional interested parties in the area to notify. 
 
 See enclosed Tab A – Area of Notification Map and Notification List. 
 
  
 Notice of Virtual Neighborhood Meeting 
 
On January 2, 2024 , a Notice of Virtual Neighborhood Meeting  was mailed by the 
applicant, Gammage & Burnham, PLC, via first-class mail to those on the notification list. 
The letter included a brief description of the request; site location; aerial map; site plan; 
virtual neighborhood meeting attendance information; and contact information for the 
applicant. 
 
See enclosed Tab B - Notice of Virtual Neighborhood Meeting Letter. 
 
 
 Citizen Participation 
 
Throughout the notification process, the Applicant has not received any comments from 
the public. 
 
 
II. Summary of Neighborhood Meeting 
A virtual neighborhood meeting was held on Tuesday, January 16, 2024 at 6:00 p.m. No 
members of the public attended the meeting. The meeting concluded at 6:30 p.m. 
  
Should any persons and/or agencies contact the Applicant to  express any concerns, 
issues, or problems they may have with the proposal in advance of public hearing , the 
Applicant will provide those comments to the Navajo County Planner that is assigned to 
this project.

January 2, 2024 
 
 
Notice of Virtual Neighborhood Meeting 
 
Dear Neighbor:  
 
We represent Rim Country RVP, LLC (“Owner”)  with respect to their 38 -space RV Park 
known as the Rim Country RV Park (“Park”). The Park is located at 3754 Rainbow Lake Drive, 3796 
Rainbow Lake Drive, and 3629 State Route 260 (“Property”). Please see enclosed Aerial Map. On behalf 
of the Owner, we will soon be filing applications with Navajo County to request a Zone Change and 
Special Use Permit (“SUP”) on the Property (“Applications”). The purpose of the Application s is to 
allow for the existing RV Park use and for expansion of the Park to add 28 new RV spaces. 
 
Most of the Property is currently zoned Commercial-Residential (C-R), with a small, triangular 
shaped portion zoned Multiple-Family Residential (R-2). Please see enclosed Aerial Zoning Map. We 
are requesting that this R-2 portion be rezoned to C-R to match the rest of the Park. Additionally, we 
are requesting a SUP for the RV Park use on the entire Property to allow for the expansion of the 
Park. As shown on the enclosed Aerial Map, most of the 38 existing RV lots are located on two of the 
three properties within the Park. The third property, located at 3796 Rainbow Lake Drive , is mostly 
vacant. The proposed Park expansion will take place on this third property (“Expansion Site”). 
 
As shown on the enclosed Site Plan, the 28 new RV spaces are all located on the Expansion 
Site. The southeast corner of the Expansion Site will be preserved as open space with the existing 
community building to remain . The existing road located on the western side of the Park will be 
extended to provide access to the 28 new lots. This existing road provides access to both Rainbow 
Lake Drive to the west and State Route 260 to the east with vehicle gates at each driveway.  The 
existing 38 spaces and access roads in the Park will remain in place.  
 
If you are interested in learning more about the Application, we welcome you to attend our 
virtual neighborhood meeting on Tuesday, January 16, 2024 at 6:00 PM.  To attend the virtual 
meeting, you can join via the link on our project website https://www.gblaw.com/rimcountry-rv/.  
 
TELEPHONE (602) 256-0566 
FACSIMILE (602) 256-4475

I. INTRODUCTION 
 
The Rim Country RV Park is an existing RV park located at 3754 Rainbow Lake 
Drive in Navajo County near Lakeside, Arizona. The site is located onto parcel 
numbers 212-43-001, 212-18-078C, and 212-18-035A. The existing RV park is 
mostly contained to parcel number 212-43-001 and includes 35 RV spaces and an 
office building. The owner of this RV park intends to expand this RV park on parcel 
number 212-18-035A with an additional 28 RV spaces. A detention basin will be 
constructed on this parcel to manage the increase in stormwater runoff from this 
proposed development. 
 
The purpose of this report is to provide the results of the preliminary drainage 
analysis that was performed, estimating the anticipated flows for the 2-year, 10-
year, 50-year and 100-year storm events for the proposed conditions and provide 
calculations for handling these flows.  
 
 
 
Figure 1. Vicinity Map 
 
 
SITE
 
 LAKESIDE

<!-- page 195 -->
Page 2 
 
II. METHODOLOGY 
 
The analysis methodology was based on the rational method, shown in Equation 1 below. 
The rational method was used to estimate peak flow rates for the storm events. 
Runoff coefficients for the site were determined using the ADOT Highway 
Drainage Design Manual-Volume 2. 
 
Equation 1. The Rational Method 
 
Q=C*I*A 
 
Q= Peak Flow Rate (cfs) 
C= Runoff Coefficient (C) 
I= Rainfall Intensity (inches per hour) 
A= Area (Acres) 
 
The peak flow rates were estimated using the AutoCAD Civil 3D “Hydraflow 
Hydrographs Extension” to route the flows through the site and to estimate the 
peak flows for the 2-year, 10-year, 50-year and 100-year events at specific 
concentration points. Rainfall data was determined using the NOAA Atlas 14 
website for Atlas 14, Volume 1, Version 5 near the project site.  
 
Autodesk’s Hydraflow Hydrographs Extension was used to create hydrographs. 
Hydrographs were then used to route flows through the site.  
 
The analysis developed flow rates for the pre and post flow rates for the 2, 10, 50 
and 100-year events for the project.  
 
The drainage area was delineated from recent mapping for the site and from field 
observations.  
 
 
III. EXISTING CONDITIONS 
 
The site generally drains from the Southeast to the Northwest in the direction of 
Rainbow Lake Road. The runoff then flows North, along the East side of Rainbow 
Lake Road to a detention basin that was built as a part of the U-Haul development. 
Figure 2 shows how the existing drainage flows across the site with flow arrows 
placed perpendicular to the existing contours. 
 
A drainage area was delineated for the site with the outflow point near the 
Northwest corner of the property. Figure 3 in the appendix shows the existing 
drainage area that was analyzed. 
 
A weighted runoff coefficient “C” was determined for the existing site. To 
determine the weighted runoff coefficient, each area with a different surface 
material was assigned a C-value. It was determined that a C-value of 0.40 would

<!-- page 196 -->
Page 3 
 
be used for the areas with native grasses, 0.70 was used for the areas of existing 
gravel or cinder roads/driveways, and 0.95 was used for any impervious surfaces. 
 
Due to the size of the drainage areas that were analyzed, the minimum time of 
concentration of 10 minutes was used for this analysis. 
 
The existing peak flows leaving the site were determined and are shown below. 
 
• 2-year = 29.6 cfs 
• 10-year = 46.0 cfs 
• 50-year = 62.4 cfs 
• 100-year = 69.9 cfs 
 
Figure 2. Existing Site 
 
 
 
 
IV. PROPOSED CONDITIONS 
 
Site

<!-- page 197 -->
Page 4 
 
The proposed improvements to the site include the addition of 28 RV spaces 
across 2.28 acres. New gravel roads will be built in to provide access to each lot 
and fire department access around the site. The proposed development will 
require a detention basin to reduce the increased flows caused by the 
development. This basin will outlet to the Northwest and allow water to flow in the 
same direction that it historically has at a similar flow rate to the existing 
conditions. Using the Civil 3D Hydrographs Extension, the required storage of the 
detention basin was estimated to be 6,477 cubic feet. There is sufficient room near 
the Northwest corner of the property to build a detention basin of this size. 
 
Two drainage areas were analyzed for the post-development conditions. Drainage 
area 1 consists of the area that will flow into the proposed detention basin before 
reaching the outfall and drainage area 2 is what will reach the outfall without 
passing through the proposed detention basin. These post-development drainage 
areas are shown in Figure 4 in the appendix. 
 
The post-development flows were estimated and are shown below. 
 
• 2-year = 29.9 cfs 
• 10-year = 46.5 cfs 
• 50-year = 63.1 cfs 
• 100-year = 70.7 cfs 
The post development flows show an overall increase in runoff from the site. This 
increase will be mitigated through the detention basin. Further analysis will be 
completed for the final drainage report to provide additional information about the 
detention basin outlet structure to prove that the overall flows from the post-
development site have been reduced to be equal to or less than the existing flows. 
 
 
VI. SUMMARY 
 
The expansion of the existing Rim Country RV Park will cause an overall increase 
in stormwater runoff from the site. A new detention basin will be constructed in the 
Northwest corner of the property to control flows before they enter the existing 
roadside ditch along Rainbow Lake Drive. The pre- and post-development flows 
were approximated to determine the increase that would be caused by this 
development. The proposed site does provide sufficient area for a detention basin 
in an effective location to mitigate the increase in runoff.

NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT
STAFF REPORT
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Hearing Date: June 25 th, 2024
Case No: ZC 24-001/SUP 24-004
Action: Zone Change and Special Use Permit
Applicant/Owner: Gammage & Burnham PLC/ Rim Country RVP, LLC
Project Name: Rim Country RV Park Expansion
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Zone Change. Staff also recommends approval
of the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions of this Staff Report. The Navajo County Planning
and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to recommend approval of the Zone Change, as well as
Special Use Permit.
Location: The property is located north of Pinetop/Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive, on APNs
#212-18-035A, 212-43-001, and 212-18-078C.
Size: 4.48 acres
Supervisor District: V
Zoning: Approximately 0.33ac of APN # 212-18-035A is R-2, pending Zone Change to C-R
(Commercial-Residential). The remainder of the parcels are zoned C-R.
Property Owners: Rim Country RVP, LLC
Applicant: Gammage & Burnham, PLC
Representative: Gammage & Burnham, PLC
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:
A request for a Zone Change from R-2 to Commercial-Residential (C-R) on an approximately 0.33-acre
portion of an approximately 2.28-acre parcel, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive.
A request for a Special Use Permit to permit an RV Park with 38 existing spaces and an expansion to
add a further 28 spaces, for a total of 66 spaces. The existing and expanded park will be approximately
4.76-acres in size, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive.

<!-- page 207 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e2 | 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:
Located directly north of this property lies U-Hall Self Storage facility, with an RV/Mobile Home Park,
automotive repair shop, and vacant land across from State Route 260. A storage facility, as well as the
Piney Slope subdivision, are located northeast/east, across State Route 260. Rainbow Forest RV Park is
located directly to the south of this property, and there are multi-family zoned lots further south. Lastly,
U-Hall RV, Boat and Self-Storage, as well as a mix of single and multi-family zoned lots are located to
the west.
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:
Currently, there is an existing, 38-space RV park occupying APNs #212-43-001, and 212-18-078C. This
is currently a legal, non-conforming use of the parcel, and the applicant seeks to bring those parcels into
compliance, in order to expand the RV Park to APN #212-18-035A.
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:
The proposed project will have the following:
 An additional 28 new RV lots, for a total of 66 RV spaces.
 6ft. solid fencing surrounding the property.
 Improved emergency access along Rainbow Lake Drive.
 Extended gravel driveway for access to the 28 new lots.
 Open, undeveloped areas.
ZONING DISTRICTS:
Current:
ARTICLE 13 - (R-2) MULTIPLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1301 – Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for multiple-family housing along with
other types of high-density housing in locations which are suitable and appropriate taking into
consideration existing conditions, including present use of land, future land use needs, and the
availability of public utilities. The minimum lot required is six thousand (6,000) square feet in
area and the minimum lot area required for each dwelling unit is two thousand (2,000) square
feet. Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include multiple-family dwellings and other
types of high-density housing, orphanages, rest homes, nursing homes and convents.
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1501 – Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets
or highways.  Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale

<!-- page 208 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e3 | 
commerce, commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature.
Proposed:
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1501 – Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets
or highways.  Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale
commerce, commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature.
ZONING ORDINANCE:
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments
Section 2001 – Special Uses
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further
prohibited by this Article.
29. Recreation Vehicle Parks, subject to all the regulations applicable to RV parks,
specified in the use regulations for the “R-3’ zoning district.
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:
Comprehensive Plan
Land Use Element
Goal 4: Provide equal opportunity for safe, decent, sanitary, and affordable housing for all
residents of the County and provide a range of residential uses that offer housing
opportunities for all County residents.
Policy 4-D: Consider developing standards for development density that reflect the needs
and goals of existing communities, whether incorporated or unincorporated, e.g. medium
to high-density residential zoning for Community Villages and Rural Edge areas and low
to medium-density for developing rural areas.
- The proposed development is located adjacent to an incorporated community,
Pinetop/Lakeside, which is intended to provide higher density residential
developments, with a mix of related commercial, industrial, and institutional
uses extending from main thoroughfares.

<!-- page 209 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e4 | 
 Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging development.
Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses
including, but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping.
- This project currently has a 6ft solid fence surrounding the property,
including the proposed expansion area, providing a buffer between any
different land uses.
Policy 6-C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and
industrial uses.
- This property is located just off State Route 260, with emergency access
routes along Rainbow Lake Drive, which are both paved, county-maintained
roadways. Additionally, the applicant will be required to provide a
Geotechnical Report for their access to, and within, the property. This will
ensure there is appropriate access for any emergency vehicles, as well as
guests.
Goal 7 : Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and
technology uses, where appropriate, to increase employment opportunities.
Policy 7-A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development so as to attract
and locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or other appropriately
surfaced access and are easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities
and cities, and in communities with air and/or rail access.
- The subject property is located along Rainbow Lake Drive, with access along
State Route 260, and sits in close adjacency to the incorporated community
of Pinetop/Lakeside.
Policy 7-D: Encourage small, specialty enterprises that support recreation and tourist
activities near the National Forest, the Petrified Forest National Park, the Homolovi State
Park, and the Native American Reservations.
- Recreational Vehicle Parks are considered specialty enterprises, and the
proposed project is located within the Apache-Sitgreaves National Forest.
Goal 8: Reduce the danger from fire for all residents living in a wildland urban interface
or near a National Forest boundary.
Policy 8-C: Discourage high-density development in areas where fire protection and
other emergency services are not readily available.
- The proposed property is located within the Timber-Mesa Fire and Medical
District and this District has provided a will-serve letter for this proposal.

<!-- page 210 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e5 | 
Policy 8-D: Continue to require multiple access routes into new large subdivisions and
other major developments for emergency purposes and to improve traffic circulation
pursuant to the Subdivision Regulations & Requirements.
- This project contains two points of access, a main entrance along State Route
260, and an emergency ingress/egress entrance along Rainbow Lake Drive.
Policy 8-E: Continue the review by the Fire Districts in the approval of subdivisions and
development projects within their respective jurisdictions.
- The Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District has reviewed this proposal and
has provided a will-serve letter for the property.
 Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner.
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the
County where services, including water and sewer systems, paved right-of-way, and
schools, are available or can be created with new developments.
- The proposal is located along State Route 260, which is a paved ADOT Right-
of-Way. The applicant has indicated they are currently served by the local
utility companies in the area, including electricity, water, and sewage.
Policy 9-D: Encourage in-fill opportunities in the County to capitalize on existing
infrastructure and reduce growth-related costs.
- The proposed expansion will fill in a vacant, adjacent lot, and will utilize the
existing utility infrastructure.
Circulation Element
Goal 1:  Provide a range of circulation options that are safe and efficient and that
complement local communities and the natural environment.
Policy 1-B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive
and efficient development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses,
complement the character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate
impacts on the natural environment.
- The proposed use is located near a number of commercially zoned lots and
has adjacency to a number of Recreational Vehicle Parks, as well as RV Self-
Storage facilities.
 Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property.
Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and
legal access for all properties.

<!-- page 211 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e6 | 
- The applicant has access from State Route 260 and will be required to provide
a Geotechnical Report to ensure safe access to, and within, the property.
Additionally, there is an existing emergency access gate and driveway along
Rainbow Lake Drive.
Analysis:
The request for a Zone Change, as well as a Special Use Permit, meets the goals and policies of the
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan as described above.
The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan encourages: development density that reflects the needs and
goals of existing communities, buffers between different land uses, appropriately surfaced access to
commercial uses, placement of development in paved areas near established communities, encouraging
specialty enterprises that support recreation and tourism near, or within, the National Forest, high-
density development only where fire protection and emergency services are available, multiple access
routes into major developments, review from the Fire District for development projects, and supporting
development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses. This project meets all the
aforementioned relevant goals and policies of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan.
As of June 6th, 2024, Planning and Development Services Staff have not received any comments in favor
of, nor against, the proposed project.
FINDINGS OF FACT:
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes
and Article 29.
This item was posted in the May 29th, 2024 edition of The Holbrook Tribune.  Postcards were
mailed to neighbors within 300 feet of the property and the subject property was posted with public
hearing notices.
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan.
The proposed project meets multiple goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan, as noted
within this report.
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the
property is located.
The proposed use is compatible with permissible uses within the C-R zoning district.
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the
vicinity of the proposed use.
The proposed use is compatible with nearby uses, which are primarily commercial and multiple-
family residential in nature.
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.
