PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Zone Change. The
Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to recommend approval of the Zone
Change on the May 16th, 2024, Commission Hearing.
On May 16th, 2024, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to recommend
approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions listed in this staff report. Navajo County Planning
and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the following
conditions:
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not limited to
building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits.
2. All corners shall be according to vision triangle rules and regulations.  No building or fencing
shall obstruct the view of traffic.
3. No building or structure shall be occupied prior to complete compliance with all appropriate
Building Division requirements.
4. All lighting on the property shall be shielded to reflect downward, be motion-activated, and be
in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.
5. A Site Lighting Plan, prepared by an Electrical Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona
(required by code for commercial projects), shall be submitted and approved if lighting is
installed onsite.

<!-- page 213 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e8 | 
6. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this Permit.
7. A fence, of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director, shall be
constructed around the boundary of the property.
8. A Geotechnical Report for access to, and within, the property shall be submitted and approved.
9. A Grading and Drainage Plan shall be submitted and approved prior to any construction.
10. A Final Drainage Study, which meets the Navajo County Drainage Policy, shall be submitted,
and approved prior to construction.
11. A water system design, meeting the requirements of the Timber-Mesa Fire and Medical District,
shall be submitted, and approved.
12. The applicant shall connect to an existing water system or create a new water system, which is
approved by ADEQ, to supply drinking water to the development.
13. The applicant shall connect to a wastewater system or create a new wastewater treatment system,
which is approved by ADEQ, to treat and dispose of wastewater from the development.
14. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from the date of
Board approval, this permit shall become null and void.
15. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific use and
to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final inspection by the Planning
and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized structures, signifying that all
zoning and site development requirements imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied,
the Special Use Permit shall thereafter be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the
maintenance of special conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other
provisions of this title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner.
16. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning and
Development Staff on the fifth (5 th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and every five (5)
years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in compliance with all conditions
contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of compliance with these conditions, this Special
Use Permit and its privileges may be revoked.

WHEREAS, an application for a Zone Change was duly filed on February 6th, 2024 by
Gammage & Burnham, PLC, for a Zone Change from R-2 to C-R (Commercial-Residential) to
allow for an expansion from an existing 38-space RV Park to 66 spaces on a 4.76-acre parcel
located north of Pinetop/Lakeside; and
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN # 212-18-035A, Township 09 North, Range 22 East, Section 22 of the Gila and Salt River
Base and Meridian; and

Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 16th, 2024; and

and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Zone Change is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should be
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and

approves the Zone Change.

allow for an expansion from an existing 38-space RV Park to 66 spaces on a 4.76-acre parcel
located north of Pinetop/Lakeside; and
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN # 212-18-035A, Township 09 North, Range 22 East, Section 22 of the Gila and Salt River

Dear Navajo County Planning & Development Department: 
 
 We represent Rim Country RVP, LLC (the “Owner”) regarding the three properties located at 
3754 Rainbow Lake Drive, 3796 Rainbow Lake Drive, and 3629 State Route 260 . The current primary 
use of the three properties is a 38-unit mobile home park, known as the Rim Country RV Park (the “Park”). 
The entire Park is identified by the County Assessor as Assessor Parcel Nos. 212 -43-001, 212-18-035A, 
and 212-18-078C (the “Property”). On behalf of the Park, we are submitting this request to for approval 
of a Special Use Permit (“SUP”) for the entire Park to allow for the existing RV Park use and any futur e 
expansion. See attached Exhibit ‘A’: Aerial Map. An additional application has been submitted to request 
a zone change of a small, triangular portion of the Property from R-2 to C-R to allow for an approved SUP 
across the entire Park. 
 
Current Conditions 
 
  The Park is approximately 4.76 acres . Most of the Property is currently zoned Commercial -
Residential (C-R), with a small, triangular shaped portion zoned Multiple-Family Residential (R-2). See 
attached Exhibit ‘B’: Current Zoning Map. As shown on the attached Aerial Map, the majority of the 38 
total RV lots are located on two of the three properties within the park. The third property located at 3796 
Rainbow Lake Drive, also known as Navajo County Assessor Parcel Number 212 -18-035A, only 
accommodates a small portion of the 38 total RV lots. Most of the area on this site is currently being used 
as open space with one small community  clubhouse building. This site is where the proposed Park 
expansion will take place (the “Expansion Site”). 
 
Project Proposal 
 
The proposed expansion of the existing RV Park is intended to increase the number of RV lots provided. 
This expansion will only affect the previously mentioned Expansion Site located at 3796 Rainbow Lake 
TELEPHONE (602) 256-0566 
FACSIMILE (602) 256-4475 
___________________ 
WRITER’S DIRECT LINE 
(602) 256-4422

<!-- page 230 -->
Special Use Permit Letter of Explanation 
3796 Rainbow Lake Drive 
February 5, 2024 
Page 2 
 
12705.2.3390577.2 
Drive. The other two properties that make up the full Park will remain mostly unchanged. The proposed 
expansion will result in approximately 28 new RV lots. The  northwest corner of the expansion area will 
be reserved for a detention basin and the southeast corner will be preserved as open space with the existing 
community building to remain. See attached Exhibit ‘C’: Site Plan. 
 
 As shown in the Site Plan, the existing drive located on the western side of the existing Park will 
be extended to provide access to the 28 new lots. This existing drive provides access to both Rainbow 
Lake Drive to the west and State Route 260 to the east with vehicle gates at each driveway. There is 
currently no plan for there to be direct access to Rainbow Drive from the Site itself. 
 
Special Use Permit Request 
 
 In order to accommodate the expansion of the RV Park, we are requesting a Special Use Permit 
for the RV Park use across all three properties within the existing Park. The Park has existed for many 
years, functioning peacefully and successfully now as a legal non-conforming use. Additionally, we have 
submitted a zone change request from R-2 to C-R for the small triangular portion of the Park shown in the 
attached Exhibit ‘B’: Current Zoning Map. 
 
 With a zone change to C-R with an SUP, the Park will be able to expand further into the currently 
unused, open space area on the northwestern portion of the Site. This expansion would fit in with the 
existing developments in the surrounding area. As shown in the attached ‘Exhibit D’: Surrounding Uses 
Map, the Park is surrounded mostly by single -family detached residences and multiple other RV Parks 
including: 
 
- Rainbow Forest RV Park adjacent to the southern border of the Park; 
- Running Bear Mobile Resort southeast of the site off of Running Bear Rd and State Route 260; 
- Rainbow Lake Mobile Home Park south of the Park on Rainbow Lake Dr and 2nd Street; and, 
- Pete’s Retreat RV Park just south of the Rainbow Lake Mobile Home Park. 
 
Given the context of the surrounding area, the expansion of the existing RV Park use would be a very 
consistent use for the Site and surrounding community.  
 
Schedule for Development 
 
 Development of the additional RV lots would occur promptly after approval.

Subject: Will-Serve Letter - Frontier Communications re RIM Country RV Park
Stephen, 
 
Pursuant to our earlier discussion, I called Frontier Communications and spoke to “Jill” (based in 
California).  I asked for contact information/email address of the person responsible for will-serve 
letters.   
 
Jill informed me that Frontier Communications does not have a department/or person for that and 
they do not send will serve letters, or even email confirmations of serviceability. 
 
What she would tell me verbally was that, as of the current date, phone service is available with no 
issues.  As far as Internet service, there are no issues, and they have available ports.  However, as the 
ports fill up, there might not be any availability when we need it.  Internet service consists of 6 
megabytes per second – DSL service.

Re: Rim Country RV Park 
s NNITARIIMTRIC  
To Whom It May Concern, 
We have been asked to provide a "Letter of Serviceability"  concerning parcels 212-18-035A, 212-18-
078C and 212-43-001. 
It is our understanding that the three parcels listed above are currently  served by sewer through a 
private lift station located on the parcel listed as 212-43-001. 
If the desire for the property owners is to change  the current connection location we would need more 
information as to the fact that sewer is only available along Highway 260. Any change would require the 
property owner(s) to extend the sewer line to the desired location at their cost since this would only 
benefit the property owner(s). 
It has also been brought to our understanding  that there is a planned expansion of this RV Park (55% 
increase), which will also increase the capacity fees for this property to be paid by the owner(s) before 
additional loading is added to our system. We cannot quote a price for the additional  charges until we 
review the plans that show the planned expansion. 
In summary, the District does currently provide serviceability  for the Rim Country RV Park, but any 
changes will need to be reviewed  before final approval can be issued. 
If there are any other questions, please let me know. 
Sincerely,

January 2, 2024 
 
 
Notice of Virtual Neighborhood Meeting 
 
Dear Neighbor:  
 
We represent Rim Country RVP, LLC (“Owner”)  with respect to their 38 -space RV Park 
known as the Rim Country RV Park (“Park”). The Park is located at 3754 Rainbow Lake Drive, 3796 
Rainbow Lake Drive, and 3629 State Route 260 (“Property”). Please see enclosed Aerial Map. On behalf 
of the Owner, we will soon be filing applications with Navajo County to request a Zone Change and 
Special Use Permit (“SUP”) on the Property (“Applications”). The purpose of the Application s is to 
allow for the existing RV Park use and for expansion of the Park to add 28 new RV spaces. 
 
Most of the Property is currently zoned Commercial-Residential (C-R), with a small, triangular 
shaped portion zoned Multiple-Family Residential (R-2). Please see enclosed Aerial Zoning Map. We 
are requesting that this R-2 portion be rezoned to C-R to match the rest of the Park. Additionally, we 
are requesting a SUP for the RV Park use on the entire Property to allow for the expansion of the 
Park. As shown on the enclosed Aerial Map, most of the 38 existing RV lots are located on two of the 
three properties within the Park. The third property, located at 3796 Rainbow Lake Drive , is mostly 
vacant. The proposed Park expansion will take place on this third property (“Expansion Site”). 
 
As shown on the enclosed Site Plan, the 28 new RV spaces are all located on the Expansion 
Site. The southeast corner of the Expansion Site will be preserved as open space with the existing 
community building to remain . The existing road located on the western side of the Park will be 
extended to provide access to the 28 new lots. This existing road provides access to both Rainbow 
Lake Drive to the west and State Route 260 to the east with vehicle gates at each driveway.  The 
existing 38 spaces and access roads in the Park will remain in place.  
 
If you are interested in learning more about the Application, we welcome you to attend our 
virtual neighborhood meeting on Tuesday, January 16, 2024 at 6:00 PM.  To attend the virtual 
meeting, you can join via the link on our project website https://www.gblaw.com/rimcountry-rv/.

Page 1 
 
I. INTRODUCTION 
 
The Rim Country RV Park is an existing RV park located at 3754 Rainbow Lake 
Drive in Navajo County near Lakeside, Arizona. The site is located onto parcel 
numbers 212-43-001, 212-18-078C, and 212-18-035A. The existing RV park is 
mostly contained to parcel number 212-43-001 and includes 35 RV spaces and an 
office building. The owner of this RV park intends to expand this RV park on parcel 
number 212-18-035A with an additional 28 RV spaces. A detention basin will be 
constructed on this parcel to manage the increase in stormwater runoff from this 
proposed development. 
 
The purpose of this report is to provide the results of the preliminary drainage 
analysis that was performed, estimating the anticipated flows for the 2-year, 10-
year, 50-year and 100-year storm events for the proposed conditions and provide 
calculations for handling these flows.  
 
 
 
Figure 1. Vicinity Map 
 
 
SITE
 
 LAKESIDE

<!-- page 260 -->
Page 2 
 
II. METHODOLOGY 
 
The analysis methodology was based on the rational method, shown in Equation 1 below. 
The rational method was used to estimate peak flow rates for the storm events. 
Runoff coefficients for the site were determined using the ADOT Highway 
Drainage Design Manual-Volume 2. 
 
Equation 1. The Rational Method 
 
Q=C*I*A 
 
Q= Peak Flow Rate (cfs) 
C= Runoff Coefficient (C) 
I= Rainfall Intensity (inches per hour) 
A= Area (Acres) 
 
The peak flow rates were estimated using the AutoCAD Civil 3D “Hydraflow 
Hydrographs Extension” to route the flows through the site and to estimate the 
peak flows for the 2-year, 10-year, 50-year and 100-year events at specific 
concentration points. Rainfall data was determined using the NOAA Atlas 14 
website for Atlas 14, Volume 1, Version 5 near the project site.  
 
Autodesk’s Hydraflow Hydrographs Extension was used to create hydrographs. 
Hydrographs were then used to route flows through the site.  
 
The analysis developed flow rates for the pre and post flow rates for the 2, 10, 50 
and 100-year events for the project.  
 
The drainage area was delineated from recent mapping for the site and from field 
observations.  
 
 
III. EXISTING CONDITIONS 
 
The site generally drains from the Southeast to the Northwest in the direction of 
Rainbow Lake Road. The runoff then flows North, along the East side of Rainbow 
Lake Road to a detention basin that was built as a part of the U-Haul development. 
Figure 2 shows how the existing drainage flows across the site with flow arrows 
placed perpendicular to the existing contours. 
 
A drainage area was delineated for the site with the outflow point near the 
Northwest corner of the property. Figure 3 in the appendix shows the existing 
drainage area that was analyzed. 
 
A weighted runoff coefficient “C” was determined for the existing site. To 
determine the weighted runoff coefficient, each area with a different surface 
material was assigned a C-value. It was determined that a C-value of 0.40 would

<!-- page 261 -->
Page 3 
 
be used for the areas with native grasses, 0.70 was used for the areas of existing 
gravel or cinder roads/driveways, and 0.95 was used for any impervious surfaces. 
 
Due to the size of the drainage areas that were analyzed, the minimum time of 
concentration of 10 minutes was used for this analysis. 
 
The existing peak flows leaving the site were determined and are shown below. 
 
• 2-year = 29.6 cfs 
• 10-year = 46.0 cfs 
• 50-year = 62.4 cfs 
• 100-year = 69.9 cfs 
 
Figure 2. Existing Site 
 
 
 
 
IV. PROPOSED CONDITIONS 
 
Site

<!-- page 262 -->
Page 4 
 
The proposed improvements to the site include the addition of 28 RV spaces 
across 2.28 acres. New gravel roads will be built in to provide access to each lot 
and fire department access around the site. The proposed development will 
require a detention basin to reduce the increased flows caused by the 
development. This basin will outlet to the Northwest and allow water to flow in the 
same direction that it historically has at a similar flow rate to the existing 
conditions. Using the Civil 3D Hydrographs Extension, the required storage of the 
detention basin was estimated to be 6,477 cubic feet. There is sufficient room near 
the Northwest corner of the property to build a detention basin of this size. 
 
Two drainage areas were analyzed for the post-development conditions. Drainage 
area 1 consists of the area that will flow into the proposed detention basin before 
reaching the outfall and drainage area 2 is what will reach the outfall without 
passing through the proposed detention basin. These post-development drainage 
areas are shown in Figure 4 in the appendix. 
 
The post-development flows were estimated and are shown below. 
 
• 2-year = 29.9 cfs 
• 10-year = 46.5 cfs 
• 50-year = 63.1 cfs 
• 100-year = 70.7 cfs 
The post development flows show an overall increase in runoff from the site. This 
increase will be mitigated through the detention basin. Further analysis will be 
completed for the final drainage report to provide additional information about the 
detention basin outlet structure to prove that the overall flows from the post-
development site have been reduced to be equal to or less than the existing flows. 
 
 
VI. SUMMARY 
 
The expansion of the existing Rim Country RV Park will cause an overall increase 
in stormwater runoff from the site. A new detention basin will be constructed in the 
Northwest corner of the property to control flows before they enter the existing 
roadside ditch along Rainbow Lake Drive. The pre- and post-development flows 
were approximated to determine the increase that would be caused by this 
development. The proposed site does provide sufficient area for a detention basin 
in an effective location to mitigate the increase in runoff.

Subject: “Will Serve” Letter for provided fire services 
Dear Mr. cooper,  
Please accept this document as a “Will Serve Letter” in regard to the proposed development and 
expansion of Rim Country RV Park, APN #212-18-035A.  The property in question is located 
within the legal boundaries of the Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District (District).  
Subsequently, the development will be required to meet the minimum requirements of the 2018 
International Fire Code as adopted by the district.  District officials will have to meet with the 
members of the development team should rezoning be approved by the county and this 
requirement is understood by all parties involved, discussion on infrastructure, access, water 
systems etc will need to be discussed. The district will issue notice of final approval upon 
completion of the proposed development.  Any changes to the proposed development must be 
approved by the district prior to construction. 
If there are any questions, please feel free to contact me for further information.   
 
Thank you, 
 
JD Pepper  
Fire Marshal 
Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District

NAVAJO N
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT
STAFF REPORT
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Hearing Date: June 25 th, 2024
Case No: ZC 24-001/SUP 24-004
Action: Zone Change and Special Use Permit
Applicant/Owner: Gammage & Burnham PLC/ Rim Country RVP, LLC
Project Name: Rim Country RV Park Expansion
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Zone Change. Staff also recommends approval
of the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions of this Staff Report. The Navajo County Planning
and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to recommend approval of the Zone Change, as well as
Special Use Permit.
Location: The property is located north of Pinetop/Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive, on APNs
#212-18-035A, 212-43-001, and 212-18-078C.
Size: 4.48 acres
Supervisor District: V
Zoning: Approximately 0.33ac of APN # 212-18-035A is R-2, pending Zone Change to C-R
(Commercial-Residential). The remainder of the parcels are zoned C-R.
Property Owners: Rim Country RVP, LLC
Applicant: Gammage & Burnham, PLC
Representative: Gammage & Burnham, PLC
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:
A request for a Zone Change from R-2 to Commercial-Residential (C-R) on an approximately 0.33-acre
portion of an approximately 2.28-acre parcel, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive.
A request for a Special Use Permit to permit an RV Park with 38 existing spaces and an expansion to
add a further 28 spaces, for a total of 66 spaces. The existing and expanded park will be approximately
4.76-acres in size, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive.

<!-- page 274 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e2 | 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:
Located directly north of this property lies U-Hall Self Storage facility, with an RV/Mobile Home Park,
automotive repair shop, and vacant land across from State Route 260. A storage facility, as well as the
Piney Slope subdivision, are located northeast/east, across State Route 260. Rainbow Forest RV Park is
located directly to the south of this property, and there are multi-family zoned lots further south. Lastly,
U-Hall RV, Boat and Self-Storage, as well as a mix of single and multi-family zoned lots are located to
the west.
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:
Currently, there is an existing, 38-space RV park occupying APNs #212-43-001, and 212-18-078C. This
is currently a legal, non-conforming use of the parcel, and the applicant seeks to bring those parcels into
compliance, in order to expand the RV Park to APN #212-18-035A.
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:
The proposed project will have the following:
 An additional 28 new RV lots, for a total of 66 RV spaces.
 6ft. solid fencing surrounding the property.
 Improved emergency access along Rainbow Lake Drive.
 Extended gravel driveway for access to the 28 new lots.
 Open, undeveloped areas.
ZONING DISTRICTS:
Current:
ARTICLE 13 - (R-2) MULTIPLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1301 – Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for multiple-family housing along with
other types of high-density housing in locations which are suitable and appropriate taking into
consideration existing conditions, including present use of land, future land use needs, and the
availability of public utilities. The minimum lot required is six thousand (6,000) square feet in
area and the minimum lot area required for each dwelling unit is two thousand (2,000) square
feet. Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include multiple-family dwellings and other
types of high-density housing, orphanages, rest homes, nursing homes and convents.
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1501 – Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets
or highways.  Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale

<!-- page 275 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e3 | 
commerce, commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature.
Proposed:
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1501 – Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets
or highways.  Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale
commerce, commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature.
ZONING ORDINANCE:
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments
Section 2001 – Special Uses
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further
prohibited by this Article.
29. Recreation Vehicle Parks, subject to all the regulations applicable to RV parks,
specified in the use regulations for the “R-3’ zoning district.
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:
Comprehensive Plan
Land Use Element
Goal 4: Provide equal opportunity for safe, decent, sanitary, and affordable housing for all
residents of the County and provide a range of residential uses that offer housing
opportunities for all County residents.
Policy 4-D: Consider developing standards for development density that reflect the needs
and goals of existing communities, whether incorporated or unincorporated, e.g. medium
to high-density residential zoning for Community Villages and Rural Edge areas and low
to medium-density for developing rural areas.
- The proposed development is located adjacent to an incorporated community,
Pinetop/Lakeside, which is intended to provide higher density residential
developments, with a mix of related commercial, industrial, and institutional
uses extending from main thoroughfares.

<!-- page 276 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e4 | 
 Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging development.
Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses
including, but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping.
- This project currently has a 6ft solid fence surrounding the property,
including the proposed expansion area, providing a buffer between any
different land uses.
Policy 6-C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and
industrial uses.
- This property is located just off State Route 260, with emergency access
routes along Rainbow Lake Drive, which are both paved, county-maintained
roadways. Additionally, the applicant will be required to provide a
Geotechnical Report for their access to, and within, the property. This will
ensure there is appropriate access for any emergency vehicles, as well as
guests.
Goal 7 : Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and
technology uses, where appropriate, to increase employment opportunities.
Policy 7-A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development so as to attract
and locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or other appropriately
surfaced access and are easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities
and cities, and in communities with air and/or rail access.
- The subject property is located along Rainbow Lake Drive, with access along
State Route 260, and sits in close adjacency to the incorporated community
of Pinetop/Lakeside.
Policy 7-D: Encourage small, specialty enterprises that support recreation and tourist
activities near the National Forest, the Petrified Forest National Park, the Homolovi State
Park, and the Native American Reservations.
- Recreational Vehicle Parks are considered specialty enterprises, and the
proposed project is located within the Apache-Sitgreaves National Forest.
Goal 8: Reduce the danger from fire for all residents living in a wildland urban interface
or near a National Forest boundary.
Policy 8-C: Discourage high-density development in areas where fire protection and
other emergency services are not readily available.
- The proposed property is located within the Timber-Mesa Fire and Medical
District and this District has provided a will-serve letter for this proposal.

<!-- page 277 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e5 | 
Policy 8-D: Continue to require multiple access routes into new large subdivisions and
other major developments for emergency purposes and to improve traffic circulation
pursuant to the Subdivision Regulations & Requirements.
- This project contains two points of access, a main entrance along State Route
260, and an emergency ingress/egress entrance along Rainbow Lake Drive.
Policy 8-E: Continue the review by the Fire Districts in the approval of subdivisions and
development projects within their respective jurisdictions.
- The Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District has reviewed this proposal and
has provided a will-serve letter for the property.
 Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner.
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the
County where services, including water and sewer systems, paved right-of-way, and
schools, are available or can be created with new developments.
- The proposal is located along State Route 260, which is a paved ADOT Right-
of-Way. The applicant has indicated they are currently served by the local
utility companies in the area, including electricity, water, and sewage.
Policy 9-D: Encourage in-fill opportunities in the County to capitalize on existing
infrastructure and reduce growth-related costs.
- The proposed expansion will fill in a vacant, adjacent lot, and will utilize the
existing utility infrastructure.
Circulation Element
Goal 1:  Provide a range of circulation options that are safe and efficient and that
complement local communities and the natural environment.
Policy 1-B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive
and efficient development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses,
complement the character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate
impacts on the natural environment.
- The proposed use is located near a number of commercially zoned lots and
has adjacency to a number of Recreational Vehicle Parks, as well as RV Self-
Storage facilities.
 Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property.
Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and
legal access for all properties.

<!-- page 278 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e6 | 
- The applicant has access from State Route 260 and will be required to provide
a Geotechnical Report to ensure safe access to, and within, the property.
Additionally, there is an existing emergency access gate and driveway along
Rainbow Lake Drive.
Analysis:
The request for a Zone Change, as well as a Special Use Permit, meets the goals and policies of the
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan as described above.
The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan encourages: development density that reflects the needs and
goals of existing communities, buffers between different land uses, appropriately surfaced access to
commercial uses, placement of development in paved areas near established communities, encouraging
specialty enterprises that support recreation and tourism near, or within, the National Forest, high-
density development only where fire protection and emergency services are available, multiple access
routes into major developments, review from the Fire District for development projects, and supporting
development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses. This project meets all the
aforementioned relevant goals and policies of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan.
As of June 6th, 2024, Planning and Development Services Staff have not received any comments in favor
of, nor against, the proposed project.
FINDINGS OF FACT:
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes
and Article 29.
This item was posted in the May 29th, 2024 edition of The Holbrook Tribune.  Postcards were
mailed to neighbors within 300 feet of the property and the subject property was posted with public
hearing notices.
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan.
The proposed project meets multiple goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan, as noted
within this report.
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the
property is located.
The proposed use is compatible with permissible uses within the C-R zoning district.
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the
vicinity of the proposed use.
The proposed use is compatible with nearby uses, which are primarily commercial and multiple-
family residential in nature.
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.

<!-- page 279 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e7 | 
There should be minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of
properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.

PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Zone Change. The
Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to recommend approval of the Zone
Change on the May 16th, 2024, Commission Hearing.
On May 16th, 2024, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously voted to recommend
approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions listed in this staff report. Navajo County Planning
and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the following
conditions:
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not limited to
building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits.
2. All corners shall be according to vision triangle rules and regulations.  No building or fencing
shall obstruct the view of traffic.
3. No building or structure shall be occupied prior to complete compliance with all appropriate
Building Division requirements.
4. All lighting on the property shall be shielded to reflect downward, be motion-activated, and be
in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.
5. A Site Lighting Plan, prepared by an Electrical Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona
(required by code for commercial projects), shall be submitted and approved if lighting is
installed onsite.

<!-- page 280 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e8 | 
6. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this Permit.
7. A fence, of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director, shall be
constructed around the boundary of the property.
8. A Geotechnical Report for access to, and within, the property shall be submitted and approved.
9. A Grading and Drainage Plan shall be submitted and approved prior to any construction.
10. A Final Drainage Study, which meets the Navajo County Drainage Policy, shall be submitted,
and approved prior to construction.
11. A water system design, meeting the requirements of the Timber-Mesa Fire and Medical District,
shall be submitted, and approved.
12. The applicant shall connect to an existing water system or create a new water system, which is
approved by ADEQ, to supply drinking water to the development.
13. The applicant shall connect to a wastewater system or create a new wastewater treatment system,
which is approved by ADEQ, to treat and dispose of wastewater from the development.
14. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from the date of
Board approval, this permit shall become null and void.
15. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific use and
to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final inspection by the Planning
and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized structures, signifying that all
zoning and site development requirements imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied,
the Special Use Permit shall thereafter be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the
maintenance of special conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other
provisions of this title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner.
16. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning and
Development Staff on the fifth (5 th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and every five (5)
years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in compliance with all conditions
contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of compliance with these conditions, this Special
Use Permit and its privileges may be revoked.

1. Applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits.

RESOLUTION ____-24
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR RIM COUNTRY RV PARK
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on February 6 th,
2024 by Gammage & Burnham, PLC, for a Special Use Permit to permit an RV Park with 38
existing spaces and an expansion to add a further 28 spaces, for a total of 66 spaces. The existing
and expanded park will be approximately 4.76-acres in size, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow
Lake Drive.
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN#s: 212-43-001, 212-18-035A, and 212-18-078C, Township 09 North, Range 22 East, Section
22 of the Gila and Salt River Base and Meridian; and
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 16th, 2024; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should
be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  the Board of Supervisors hereby
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions:
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not
limited to building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits.
2. All corners shall be according to vision triangle rules and regulations.  No building
or fencing shall obstruct the view of traffic.
3. No building or structure shall be occupied prior to complete compliance with all
appropriate Building Division requirements.
4. All lighting on the property shall be shielded to reflect downward, be motion-
activated, and be in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.
5. A Site Lighting Plan, prepared by an Electrical Engineer, licensed in the State of
Arizona (required by code for commercial projects), shall be submitted and

<!-- page 289 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
approved.
6. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site
plan.  Any expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to
this Permit.
7. A fence, of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director,
shall be constructed around the boundary of the property.
8. A Geotechnical Report for access to, and within, the property shall be submitted and
approved.
9. A Grading and Drainage Plan shall be submitted and approved.
10. A Drainage Study, which meets the Navajo County Drainage Policy, shall be
submitted and approved.
11. A water system design, meeting the requirements of the Timber-Mesa Fire and
Medical District, shall be submitted and approved.
12. The applicant shall connect to an existing water system or create a new water system,
which is approved by ADEQ, to supply drinking water to the development.
13. The applicant shall connect to a wastewater system or create a new wastewater
treatment system, which is approved by ADEQ, to treat and dispose of wastewater
from the development.
14. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from
the date of Board approval, this permit shall become null and void.
15. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the
specific use and to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final
inspection by the Planning and Development Services Director (or designee) of any
authorized structures, signifying that all zoning and site development requirements
imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied, the Special Use Permit shall
thereafter be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the maintenance of special
conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other provisions of this
title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner.
16. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning
and Development Staff on the fifth (5th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and
every five (5) years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in
compliance with all conditions contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of
compliance with these conditions, this Special Use Permit and its privileges may be
revoked.

SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR RIM COUNTRY RV PARK
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on February 6 th,
2024 by Gammage & Burnham, PLC, for a Special Use Permit to permit an RV Park with 38
existing spaces and an expansion to add a further 28 spaces, for a total of 66 spaces. The existing
and expanded park will be approximately 4.76-acres in size, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow
Lake Drive.
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN#s: 212-43-001, 212-18-035A, and 212-18-078C, Township 09 North, Range 22 East, Section
22 of the Gila and Salt River Base and Meridian; and
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 16th, 2024; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Special Use Permit is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and
should not be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  the Board of Supervisors hereby
denies the Special Use Permit.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. d. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/25/2024  
Title: Approval of three Temporary Construction Easements
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Donald Perkins, Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: William Flake
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and acceptance of three (3) Temporary Construction Easements over and across real
property along Bourdon Ranch Road in Navajo County, for construction of drainage facilities.
Background:
Bourdon Ranch Road at Millett Swale has experienced unsafe stormwater runoff events due to the failure
of the Millett Swale Dam.  The County is working on a Capital Improvement Project to increase the size
and carrying capacity of two existing culverts conveying stormwater flows under Bourdon Ranch Road. 
The County has all the Temporary Construction Easements necessary to move forward with the
construction project, which is expected to start at the beginning of August.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the acceptance of the three Temporary Construction Easements.

Fiscal Year: 24/25
Budgeted Y/N: N
